# sillajuku

Every record you've loved — rated, cataloged, and remembered. A music platform for listeners with taste.

**Stack:** Next.js 14 (App Router) · Swift/SwiftUI (iOS) · Supabase (auth + database) · Spotify API · Tailwind CSS

**Monorepo:** `apps/web` (Next.js) · `apps/ios` (Swift — in progress) · `packages/shared` (TypeScript types)

---

## ⚠️ Current state (2026-07-15)

Features shipped as of 2026-06-08: Daily Question, preferred streaming platform, **Collections** (custom playlists, renamed) with foldable panel and per-add destination picker, Bayesian Silla Score, Discovery/Adventurousness slider. See SESSIONS.md for details. **2026-06-10:** `backfill:embeddings` ✅ complete — all ~347k non-single releases have Jina v3 embeddings. HNSW index ✅ rebuilt (2026-06-09). Hybrid semantic search ready — deploy to Vercel to activate. **2026-06-11:** Performance overhaul — homepage blank-screen fix (Suspense streaming), GIN trigram index on `releases.genres` (eliminates IO-exhausting full-table scans; required **Supabase Pro** upgrade to build — free tier Disk IO Budget was exhausted), Redis caching on album page ranking badges (5-min TTL, 10–15 queries → 1 cache hit), profile page `auth.admin.listUsers(1000)` → targeted SQL function, query limits on category-resolver and canon-suggestions. New live search dropdown: `SearchBar` component + `/api/search/suggest` endpoint (prefix match, no Jina, 10-min Redis cache) — near-instant after first query. **2026-06-14:** Local QA pass complete — 4 bugs found and fixed (ISSUE-002–006). Logo updated to transparent background. Browser language detection added (Accept-Language header + navigator.language). Onboarding improved: sidebar/footer/listen-later hidden, logo non-clickable, genre pills sorted by DB frequency, Apple Music added as streaming option (requires migration `20260615000000`), essentials prioritize Albums > EPs > Singles. **2026-06-16 (mobile):** Expo app brought up on Windows/Expo Go (fixed an SDK-56-vs-54 dependency skew in `apps/mobile/package.json`). Mobile design pass started — device-language i18n (`apps/mobile/lib/i18n/`, follows the phone's language, en/ko), login redesign (non-scroll + flower-mark logo), home navbar removed, unified `ScreenHeader` across the four tab screens. `backfill:tracklists` ✅ complete (107,778 / 115,604 = 93%). See SESSIONS.md (2026-06-16) for details + known issues. **2026-06-17 (architecture pivot):** React Native (`apps/mobile`) retired and deleted. iOS app rebuilding from scratch in Swift/SwiftUI (`apps/ios/` — Mac session). New feature decisions: OAuth-only login (Spotify recommended, Apple, Google — no email/password), simplified onboarding (name + username + rating mode + notifications; Google adds genre step), Essentials feature removed from entire app, Instinct rating mode added (pairwise comparison / Elo). See `WEB_PARITY.md` for full spec of changes to mirror on web. Two parallel tracks: Mac builds iOS app; Windows builds Elo system, DB migrations, `/api/rate/compare` endpoint, removes essentials from web, updates Spotify OAuth scopes. **2026-06-17 (session 3, Windows):** all 5 web-prep tasks built — Instinct migration (`20260617000000`, ⏳ apply via SQL editor), `lib/elo.ts`, `/api/rate/compare`, essentials removed (ProfilePanel + AlbumActions + onboarding `StepAlbums`; `Essentials.tsx`/`Pick5Modal.tsx` deleted; `pinned_albums` left unused), Spotify login scopes (`user-top-read` + `user-read-recently-played`). Typecheck clean. Remaining web parity (onboarding rewrite, Instinct album-page UI, `/rate` session, settings toggle, i18n) still open — see START HERE. **2026-06-18 (iOS tab screens):** All 5 tab screens built — `HomeView` (parallel genre carousels from `recommendable_releases`, `withTaskGroup`), `SearchView` (debounced search with 3-column grid), `RankingsView` (ranking categories list), `ActivityView` (global ratings feed with cover + score + timestamp), `ProfileView` (stats, rating mode badge, 3-column grid with score overlay, sign out). Shared: `AlbumCard` component, `Release` + `RankingCategory` models, `Date.relativeTimeString` helper. Simulator still blocked on 8GB RAM — use Xcode Previews (`#Preview` on each screen) to test without the full simulator. **2026-06-18 (session 2 — album detail + physical device):** `AlbumDetailView` built — full-width cover hero, title/artist/release type chip/year, community stats (avg ★ + count), half-star rating widget (tap to rate, tap same to clear, writes to `ratings` via upsert), tracklist section (from `tracklist` JSONB column — fetched separately on detail load, MM:SS duration). `TrackItem` model + `StarRatingView` component added. `Release` extended with `Hashable`, `tracklist`, `totalTracks`. `NavigationLink(value:)` wired into every tappable card: `HomeView` carousels, `SearchView` grid, `ProfileView` ratings grid, `ActivityView` list rows. Debug build config updated to `CODE_SIGN_STYLE = Automatic`, team `GGJ5HX3A4M` — app can now be deployed to a physical iPhone via Xcode. **2026-06-18 (iOS session 3 — UI polish + dark mode):** Auth screen polished — two oversized decorative flowers (top-right 140% screen width / bottom-left 100%, opacity 0.09, cut at physical screen edges via `ignoresSafeArea`), legal text pinned to bottom in a separate ZStack layer, slogan rendered as `Color.sjInk.opacity(0.6)`. Onboarding redesigned: 4 single-question screens (What's your name? / Create a username. / Select a rating style. / Turn on notifications.), auto-keyboard via `@FocusState` + `.onAppear`, OAuth provider name pre-filled in name field (reads `full_name` / `name` from Supabase `userMetadata`). Tab bar restructured: Search tab renamed to **Add**, Activity tab renamed to **Feed**, order changed to Home · Rankings · Add · Feed · Profile, all ViewModels hoisted to `MainTabView` as `@State` to prevent tab-switch reloads, `hasLoaded` guard in each ViewModel's `load()`. Full-screen `AppLoadingView` (flower + wordmark + `ProgressView`) replaces in-Home spinner. Dark mode: 5 adaptive color assets (`sjCream/sjInk/sjMuted/sjBorder/sjSurface` — light + dark variants in `Assets.xcassets`), `@AppStorage("appearanceMode")` + `.preferredColorScheme()` at app root, Appearance submenu in Profile `···` menu (System / Light / Dark + checkmarks). All `Color.white` → `Color.sjSurface`. Build errors fixed: removed duplicate adaptive color declarations from `Theme.swift` (Xcode 15+ auto-generates them from `.colorset` assets); fixed `providerName` reference removed from `OnboardingView.finish()`.

> **✅ SESSION CLOSE (2026-08-26, Windows, web, session 3) — "Your library across the years" trend line smoothed + made to span the full range. COMMITTED + PUSHED. Full detail in SESSIONS.md 2026-08-26 (session 3 / 3b).**
> 1. **Smooth, shape-preserving trend line** (`components/sj/TasteCharts.tsx` → `YearChart`): the 5-year moving-average trend was rendered as an angular `L`-segment polyline. New `monotonePath()` helper draws it as a **monotone cubic Hermite** spline (Fritsch–Carlson tangents) — smooth *and* non-overshooting, so it eases onto the baseline across **zero-count years** instead of ringing below the axis. Verified numerically (spike→zeros stays within `[0,H]`); `tsc`/`next lint` clean.
> 2. **Trend now spans the whole range** (follow-up): it *looked* like it stopped at the last non-empty year (e.g. 2001) because a zero-count tail mapped to `y=H`, where the SVG bottom edge + `border-b` clip it. Fixed by insetting the trend's y-map (`TREND_TOP=4`, `TREND_BOT=6` viewBox units) so a flat/zero stretch rides ~7px above the axis, visible end-to-end. Bars keep their full-height mapping.

> **✅ SESSION CLOSE (2026-08-25, research only, no code) — sillajuku passed App Store review and is awaiting release. User asked whether release timing affects long-term App Store discoverability, and what to do about wanting to release to friends now while still polishing before a "real" launch. Researched (web search across ASO industry sources + Apple developer forums), no code touched. Full detail in SESSIONS.md.**
> - **Decision context for whoever picks this up:** no evidence that a quiet friends-only release now creates any lasting penalty later — App Store ranking is continuously recalculated off rolling retention/engagement/rating signals, not a fixed launch-day score (Apple's own dev forums call the "day-1 downloads set your ceiling forever" idea an outdated myth). The one real cost of releasing quietly is forfeiting the "new app boost" (~7-day organic keyword-search visibility bump, one-time per country) — but that's irrelevant right now since friends install via direct link, not App Store search, so there's nothing to lose by using it now vs. later.
> - **Recommendation given, not yet acted on:** release to friends now under the current build; treat the "real" public launch (ASO — keywords/screenshots/Custom Product Pages, review-seeding, any community/press push) as a **separate, later, deliberately-timed event** once the planned pre-launch improvements are done — mirrors the professional soft-launch-small-then-coordinated-hard-launch pattern found in the research, not something specific to this app.
> - **No numeric study exists** tying "captured the new app boost" to success odds, or quantifying how much harder a delayed public push is — flagged honestly rather than invented. Base-rate context surfaced instead: ~0.4-0.7% of apps hit $100K/12mo revenue (GoPractice study), unconditional on launch timing.
>

> **✅ SESSION CLOSE (2026-08-18, Mac, FlowerRateControl → Liquid Glass) — User: "quick add page: the top part's buttons are not liquid glass and they are not clear (no content is visible behind them)." > 1. **`Components/FlowerRateControl.swift`'s resting-state circle was a flat, fully opaque `Circle().fill(.white)`** (plus a matching flat fill for the rated/tinted and delete-zone states) — no material at all, unlike `ScoreBadge`'s already-established `.glassEffect(...)` treatment for rated circles elsewhere in the app. Since `FlowerRateControl` is the single shared control behind every quick-rate flower (Home feed, artist page rows, Profile, Quick Add, Search), this wasn't fixable per-page — the fix belongs in the one component, and now applies everywhere that reuses it, not only Quick Add.

Applied to both the in-flow button and the popped-forward drag-duplicate (`RateGaugeView.poppedFlower`) so the control doesn't visibly switch from glass to flat the moment a drag starts.

> **✅ SESSION CLOSE (2026-08-18, Mac, launch loading screen) — User: "loading screen: create a percentage gauge + raise error if device is not connected to network." Scoped to the app-launch splash specifically (user's explicit choice over the Home-feed splash) — `RootView`'s `.loading` case in `sillajukuApp.swift`, shown while `observeAuth()` waits for Supabase's first auth event. That case was previously just `Color.white.ignoresSafeArea()` — a blank screen with nothing on it, which is also the one place a disconnected device could get stuck indefinitely (this auth check needs network for anything beyond a cached signed-out state).**
> 1. **New `Services/NetworkMonitor.swift`** — `@Observable` singleton wrapping `NWPathMonitor`, push-based (`isConnected` flips automatically the instant the path changes, no polling). Starts optimistic (`true`) so it doesn't flash a false error before the first path update arrives.
> 2. **New `LaunchLoadingView`** (private struct, bottom of `sillajukuApp.swift`) replaces the blank white case: same flower/wordmark branding as `MainTabView`'s existing `AppLoadingView`, plus a percentage-ring gauge — reuses the app's own established ring pattern from `ScoreBadge` (`Circle().trim(from:to:).stroke(..., lineCap: .round).rotationEffect(-90°)`), filled via a time-based ease (`0.92 * (1 - exp(-t/1.4))`) since there's no discrete multi-step progress to measure here, just one opaque wait — deliberately capped short of 100% so it never "lies" about being done before the real transition happens. Swaps to a "No internet connection" notice (with `wifi.slash` icon) the instant `NetworkMonitor.shared.isConnected` goes false; no retry button needed since the monitor is push-based and the underlying auth check never stopped running — reconnecting flips it back on its own.
> 3. **Live-verified in Simulator via a precisely-timed screenshot burst** (auth resolves in ~150-250ms with a cached session, too fast for a single fixed-delay screenshot to reliably catch) — caught the gauge mid-render showing a live "0%", confirmed the transition into the (unchanged) Home-feed `AppLoadingView` afterward still works. **Not live-tested: the actual offline/no-network state** — cutting real network to the Simulator would mean disabling the Mac's own WiFi, too disruptive to do mid-session. The code path (`NWPathMonitor` → `@Observable` property → `if/else` in the view body) is standard, well-precedented SwiftUI/Network-framework usage, but flagging this honestly rather than claiming a live-verified offline test that didn't happen.
> 4. **Out of scope, untouched:** `MainTabView.swift`'s `AppLoadingView` (Home-feed loading, post-auth) — user explicitly chose the launch splash only when asked which of the two loading screens this should apply to.
> 5. **`xcodebuild -scheme sillajuku -destination 'generic/platform=iOS Simulator' build` → BUILD SUCCEEDED.** Committed + pushed (`90affeb`).

> **✅ RESOLVED (2026-08-18, Mac, search_artists — closed) — `search_artists` went from ~2-4s (and briefly ~1.3s, still too slow) down to ~230ms, confirmed by both timing AND a real `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` this time, not assumed from a third pattern-match. Live-verified in Simulator: the artist page now fully settles (album list, stats, avatar, own rating) in well under 2 seconds.**
> 1. **Two rounds of index-shaped fixes (`20260818000000`, `20260818000001`) were each individually correct but didn't move the number** — re-timing after each showed no real improvement, which is why a diagnostic `_debug_explain()` helper (`20260818000002`, service_role-only, temporary) got added so the actual query plan could be read instead of guessed at a third time from pattern-matching against the sibling `search_release_groups` fix.
> 2. **The real `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` showed the true cost: a 4.39-second sequential scan of all 67,634 `artists` rows**, driven by the "has releases" gate — a condition the *original* migration's own comment claimed was "cheap," true per-invocation but not as the plan's driving access path across the whole table. The two already-fixed arms (word_similarity, the alias subquery) were confirmed working correctly in isolation in that same plan — they just never got the chance to matter, since Postgres drove the scan from the *other* AND-clause instead.
> 3. **Fix (`20260818000003`): restructured `search_artists` as a `UNION` of five independently-indexed single-condition candidate queries** instead of one big `WHERE` and hoping the planner picks the selective side. Each branch now hits its own dedicated trgm index directly; the small resulting candidate set (single digits for a real query) is *then* joined back to `artists` and checked against "has releases" — a handful of index probes instead of ~112,000. Confirmed via a second `EXPLAIN ANALYZE`: every branch now shows a `Bitmap Index Scan`, execution time 231ms (24× faster than the original 5.57s).
> 4. **`20260818000004` (not urgent, apply whenever): drops the temporary `_debug_explain()` helper** now that it's served its purpose.
> 5. **Two minor, non-blocking things noted, not fixed:** ~185ms of the remaining 231ms is the `ORDER BY`'s exact-alias-match ranking boost (still a full scan of `artist_aliases`, but only affects result *ordering*, not correctness — could be tightened with a plain btree index on `normalize_text(alias)` later if it matters). Very short queries (2 chars, e.g. "IU") still fall back to an unindexed path — pre-existing, documented caveat from the original 2026-07-06 sibling fix, unrelated to this session's work.
>
> **✅ SESSION CLOSE (2026-08-18, Mac, final follow-up) — User: "the flower should be in the right hand side. also, the badge looks very off at the moment because it is just scaled down. we need another design." Both resolved with one change: `ArtistReleaseRow` now uses a single `AlbumRateButton` for everything.**

> **▶ ACTIVE (2026-08-10 → 08-15, Windows) — video-track cleanup is DONE, so the retuned recency pipeline is unblocked. The one thing to do next: start it with `npm run pipeline:fresh`.**
> - **✅ Everything below is committed AND pushed to `origin/main` (8 commits + a merge, pushed 2026-08-15).** Working tree clean, `apps/web` typechecks clean. **Pull on the other device before touching anything** — this batch spans web, scripts, pipeline, and a migration.
> - **⚠ The 08-15 merge with the Mac's Instinct-mode removal caught a live regression — read this before touching rating writes.** Upstream replaced every rate button's direct Supabase write with the new app-wide `RatingsStore`, but `setRating` applied the score optimistically and **discarded the write error**, reintroducing the bug fixed on 07-24 (see the RECOVERED block below) in a strictly worse place: it is now the single write path for the whole app, and the optimistic value sits in a shared cache, so one rejected write would show a wrong score on *every* surface until a reload. `RatingsStore.setRating` now rolls the cache entry back and logs on failure. **If you add another write path, it must do the same.**
> - **✅ The catalog-wide video-track `--write` COMPLETED 2026-08-12** — 43,135 `video` rows deleted across 7,033 releases, 2,514 `gone` rows deliberately left alone, **0 `track_ratings` touched**. Full detail in item 1 of the 2026-07-22 block below. **This was the only thing holding the pipeline back** (it and the ingest lanes share one MusicBrainz rate limit); nothing is competing for MB now.
> - **New `newreleases` lane (daily, ON by default).** Per-artist freshness polling could never keep the catalog current: ~3 MB requests × 67.5k artists ≈ **2.3 days of MB time per cycle**. The lane inverts the question — one MB date-window search ("what came out since X?") instead of 67.5k per-artist polls — and flags only the catalog artists credited on something new, which the existing FRESHNESS path then re-polls. Measured live: **~75 of our artists get a new release per day**, so a day's recency costs ~4 minutes of MB time instead of 2.3 days. `scripts/discover-mb-newreleases.ts` (also runnable standalone: `npm run discover:newreleases:dry`). It never ingests directly — it only moves `next_check_at`.
> - **FRESHNESS ordering defect fixed.** It claimed the *most overdue* artist, which after any pause serves dormant artists before hot ones. Now tier-ordered (`hot → active → known → dormant`), using the `idx_artists_priority` index that already existed for it.
> - **`--fresh-focus` / `npm run pipeline:fresh`** = `--no-discover` + `AREA_DISCOVERY=0` + `FRESHNESS_EVERY=3`, NEWRELEASES on. Less expansion, more new releases.
> - **⏳ Still has NOT been run for real — verified stopped again on 2026-08-15** (no ingest process on this machine; the pipeline has now been down since ~2026-07-19, about 4 weeks). The blocker cleared on 08-12 and no session has started it since. Expect ~4-5 min for the first `newreleases` sweep and a few thousand artists flagged to catch up the gap, ~2-3h of re-polls. **Run `npm run pipeline:status` + `pipeline:verify` right after starting** — PIPELINE_CHECKS.md's next-due is "immediately on pipeline restart" (nothing is owed before then, which is why no check row was added on 08-12 or 08-15), and the `area`/`recency`/`reconcile` lane heartbeats should reappear.
> - **Two follow-ups the cleanup left behind, both product calls, neither blocking:** (a) deleting a middle video disc leaves the surviving discs numbered **1 and 3**, so the album page's new disc headers read "Disc 1 / Disc 3" with a visible gap — decide whether to renumber sequentially for display; (b) the 2,514 `gone` rows are reviewable in `apps/web/scripts/data/video-cleanup-report-2026-08-11.json` (11MB, **now gitignored — local to this machine only**), no ratings depend on any of them.

> **✅ RECOVERED + RESOLVED (2026-07-24, Windows) — a session that ended abruptly left three real fixes uncommitted and undocumented for ~3 weeks. Found and committed 2026-08-12; the one open question is now closed.**
> 1. **Re-rating an album was silently failing in production since 2026-05-18 — fixed by `20260724000000_rating_history_insert_policy.sql`, ✅ CONFIRMED APPLIED.** `record_rating_change()` is a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger on `ratings` that inserts into `rating_history`. That table has had RLS enabled with **only a SELECT policy** since `20260518000000_rating_history.sql` — so the trigger's insert was denied by default, raised `42501` (`insufficient_privilege`), and Postgres rejected the entire `UPDATE`. **Every score *change* failed for ~2 months**; brand-new ratings are pure INSERTs into `ratings`, fire no `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger, and were unaffected — which is why it stayed invisible. The migration adds the mirroring INSERT policy (`WITH CHECK ((SELECT auth.uid()) = user_id)`; the `ratings` UPDATE policy already guarantees `NEW.user_id = auth.uid()`). **Applied status was initially unknown** (the session that wrote it never closed) — resolved 2026-08-12 when re-running it returned **`42710: policy already exists`**, i.e. that session did apply it before dying. So the prod fix has been live since 07-24 and re-rating works. **The migration was then made idempotent** (`DROP POLICY IF EXISTS` before `CREATE`) — bare `CREATE POLICY` fails on a second run, which is a bad property in a project where migrations are applied by hand across two machines. The drop also makes it self-correcting: running it always leaves the intended rule in place regardless of what's there. **No `rating_history` backfill is needed** — the trigger aborted the whole `UPDATE`, so the score change never landed *and* no history row was written; the two tables stayed mutually consistent the entire time. Nothing to repair.
> 2. **`AlbumRateButton` no longer leaves a phantom score behind (the client half of the same bug).** `quickRate`/`saveModal` set the optimistic score, then discarded the Supabase `error` entirely — so a rating that was rejected by the RLS failure above (or any constraint/auth/network error) still *looked* saved until the next refresh. Both now capture the error, revert `shown` to its previous value, log the reason, and skip `onScoreChange`.
> 3. **`mix/[id]/layout.tsx`'s soft-404 was deliberately reverted (`fbdc0d0`'s change was wrong for mixes).** That commit added `notFound()` on an empty fetch across album/song/artist/mix/profile layouts. But mixes are privacy-scoped and the layout's `fetchMix` runs on the **anon key with no server-side session**, so *every private mix* comes back empty there — **including for its own owner, who was getting 404'd out of their own mix.** The layout is now a passthrough; the client page (which does have the session) owns the not-found decision. `generateMetadata` still does the anon fetch, so a crawler on a private mix gets the generic title/description with no name or content leak — just not a hard 404. **The album/song/artist/profile layouts were NOT touched** — they're public-by-default, so the original fix is still correct there.

> 2. **The sticker is now draggable (repositions), pinchable (resizes 0.5–2.2×), and double-tap resets both to center/default** — `@State private var stickerOffset`/`stickerScale` + `@GestureState` for the live in-flight gesture deltas, `.highPriorityGesture` on the sticker itself so it wins over the TabView's own page-swipe when a touch starts on the card (swipes starting elsewhere on the canvas still page through backgrounds normally — confirmed live, both interactions coexist correctly). A one-time "Drag or pinch the card to adjust" hint shows only while the sticker is still at its untouched default, since this isn't an obvious affordance the way it is inside Instagram's own app.
> 3. **The user's placement now actually exports** — `InstagramShare.composite` gained `stickerScale`/`stickerOffsetFraction` params (fractions of canvas size, not points, so the same values are valid at both the 1080×1920 story canvas and the 1080×1350 post canvas); `SharePreviewSheet`'s `exportImage`/`postExportImage` thread the committed (post-gesture, not live-drag) values through. **Platform limitation, not a bug:** Instagram's own pasteboard contract for the direct Stories/Reels handoff has no sticker-position key, so a custom placement only carries through to Save/More/Post — the direct-to-Instagram share always hands off a flat sticker that Instagram itself centers, same as before. Documented explicitly in `shareToInstagramStories`'s doc comment so it isn't mistaken for a regression later.
> 4. **Verified live in Simulator**, including a real macOS Simulator pinch (held Option + dragged, which Simulator maps to a mirrored two-finger pinch): dragged the card to a new position (confirmed via screenshot, hint correctly disappeared), pinched it larger (confirmed via screenshot), double-tapped to confirm it snapped back to center/default size, and swiped elsewhere on the canvas to confirm background paging (Solid Color → Gradient) still works and the sticker's custom placement persists across pages. Did not pixel-inspect an actual exported/saved image — the export math is a direct, symmetric application of the same on-screen values, not independently re-verified beyond code review.

> 1. **Recs ranked by fit, not prestige** (`app/api/taste/profile/route.ts`): pools stay prestige-gated to canon-quality (now `limit 90`, + `first_release_date`/artist `country`), then **re-ranked by `blobAffinity`** (genre + era + scene), prestige as tiebreak — so "you might like" is on-taste and differs per world instead of repeating the same canon.

Measured cosines: **r&b↔soul 0.96** (twins) vs indie-rock↔alt-rock 0.73 / k-pop↔j-pop 0.55 (distinct). `mergeSynonymWeights` is now **two-stage**: spelling-canonicalize (new `lib/taste/genreSynonyms.ts`, inert-but-correct safety net) → **embedding near-twin fold** (positive tags within `NEAR_DUP_COSINE = 0.93` collapse into the stronger anchor, soul→r&b; disliked tags never fold). Returns `{ weights, anchorOf }`; the route maps raw album/rec genres onto merged tiles (`toTile`) and expands anchors to raw spellings for the overlap query (`spellingsOf`). Stored `genre_weights` keeps raw keys (iOS). Cache `v8 → v9`.
> 3. **Tunable knob:** `NEAR_DUP_COSINE` in `lib/taste/profile.ts` (0.93) sets merge aggressiveness (↓0.90 also folds motown/quiet-storm into r&b).
> 4. **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` + `next lint` clean; merge/cosine/vocab logic checked against the real embedding artifact. **Not yet eyeballed live** (≥25-ratings gate) — worth confirming, for an r&b/soul rater, that they now read as one tile with combined avg, and that per-world recs feel personal.

> **✅ SESSION CLOSE (2026-08-12, Windows, web) — Taste map reconstructed as a cover-mosaic treemap (portfolio-piece pass). COMMITTED + PUSHED. Full detail in SESSIONS.md 2026-08-12.**
> 1. **Why:** the treemap's flat score-ramp fill was bland (world averages cluster in a narrow band → a monochrome quilt carrying no info), drill-in hard-swapped the whole box with no spatial continuity, fixed-px label thresholds pushed/clipped text, and the side panel duplicated the map as bars. Album art — a music app's richest, most-varying asset — was used for nothing.
> 2. **What changed** (`TasteGraph.tsx`, self-contained — no server/data-contract change; mosaics are built client-side from `data.albums`): each tile is now **tiled with the covers you rated in it**, grid density scaling to the tile's measured pixel size (`chooseGrid`, ResizeObserver, ~96px cells, ≤3×3, never a half-empty grid; falls back to the score gradient only when a corner has no art). Avg rating rides as a **solid numeric chip** (`spectrumFill`+`spectrumNumber`, matching the top-album badge); the score ramp is **demoted to a thin accent frame** (glanceable heat, no longer flooding the tile). Drill-in is an **origin-anchored zoom** — the sub-genre layer grows out of the clicked tile's rect (CSS `taste-zoom-in` keyframe fed the rect as CSS vars) — with a **breadcrumb** (All worlds ▸ World ▸ sub-genre) replacing the floating Back. Focus is now a **spotlight** (non-selected tiles darken via `filter`, not `opacity`). Sub-genres are **deduped by display name**. Recs **always render** with a labelled world-pool fallback ("More in {world}") so the panel is never inconsistently empty. Inspector default state is a **cover-thumbnail launcher** (was redundant bars). Tiles are `role="button"` divs (Cover renders a `<div>` — no invalid `<div>`-in-`<button>` nesting).
> 3. **i18n:** new `sj.taste.*` keys in `en.ts`+`ko.ts` (mapAllWorlds/mapMoreIn/mapOthers/mapExplore/mapShareOfLibrary); mapSub/mapHint reworded for the cover language.
> 4. **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` + `next lint` (touched files) clean. **Not yet eyeballed in a live browser** (needs a signed-in account with ≥25 ratings) — worth a hands-on pass on mosaic density across tile sizes + both themes, the zoom-from-tile feel, and cover-load performance on a busy map.

> **✅ SESSION CLOSE (2026-08-11, Mac, session 4) — Two rounds of feedback on session 3's Liquid Glass morph, both implemented and VERIFIED LIVE — and live verification caught two real bugs that a clean build would never have surfaced.**
> 1. **Round 1 (styling/layout), `Components/MorphingRateButton.swift`:** idle tint opacity 0.7 → 0.95 (reads as solid vivid blue, not blurry, while staying real glass so `glassEffectID` keeps morphing it smoothly); flower size unified to 30pt everywhere (was 90pt on the album flow, 28pt on tracks) — one consistent size app-wide, matching `FlowerRateControl`'s own existing default. `PostRatingOptionsView` gained `showHeader: Bool` so its inline (non-sheet) use can skip the redundant cover/title recap.
> 1b. **Follow-up refinement, same day: user wanted the intermediate (just-committed, pre-"Done") stage to look exactly like the real post, not a standalone badge.** `AlbumDetailView.ratingBody` now shows the actual `ratedBody(score:)` (the real `FeedCard` — avatar, username, timestamp, cover, score ring) the instant `displayScore` is non-nil, with the comment/mix/Done step appended directly below it while `showPostRatingStep` is true; "Done" just collapses that appended step, the post itself never re-renders. This made `MorphingRateButton`'s 3rd (`.rated`/`ScoreBadge`) phase and its `showsRatedState` param dead code, since the parent now swaps the whole component out for the real post before that phase would ever matter — both removed, back to a simple `idle`/`rating` component that intentionally stays showing the flower (not reverting to idle) after a commit, letting the caller's own unmount handle the transition with no visible flash back to the idle button.
> 1c. **Second follow-up, same day: user clarified the rating is still incomplete until "Done," so the UI shouldn't read as a finished, standalone post — no heart/comment icons, and it should look visually connected to the comment/mix/Done step below, not two separate stacked cards.** `FeedCard` (`Main/HomeView.swift`) gained `var isDraft: Bool = false` — skips its own like/comment action bar and its own background/corner/shadow chrome when true, so it renders as bare content a caller can embed. `AlbumDetailView.ratingBody`'s mid-flow branch now wraps `ratedBody(score:, isDraft: true)` + a `Divider` + `PostRatingOptionsView` together inside ONE shared `VStack` with a single card background/clipShape/shadow, instead of the post carrying its own separate card look. Once "Done" is tapped, the branch swaps to the plain `ratedBody(score:)` (default `isDraft: false`) — the normal, complete, standalone card with its like/comment actions restored.
> 2. **Round 2 (new feature), drag-to-delete:** `FlowerRateControl` gained `onDelete`, only offered when `currentScore != nil` (nothing to delete on a fresh rating). Dragging into a fixed top-center screen position (`RateGaugeGeometry.deleteZoneCenter`, deliberately NOT relative to the flower's own position) blurs the background (real `.regularMaterial` on the floating overlay window, not a fake static effect) and shows a trash target; releasing there deletes instead of committing a score. Wired via the same `saveModal(nil)`/"Remove Rating" paths that already existed in `AlbumRateButton`/`SongRateButton`/`ManualRatingSheet`/`TrackRatingSheet`.
> 3. **Live verification (simulator, `cliclick`/`screencapture`, real Supabase writes) found and fixed two real bugs neither `xcodebuild` nor a code read caught:**
>    - **Race condition in `AlbumDetailView.ratingBody`:** rating a fresh album morphed the flower into... the plain idle "Rate this Album" button again, not the score badge — even though the score visibly saved (community stats updated). Root cause: `viewModel.setRating` sets `userScore` (→ `displayScore`) *synchronously before* its network `await`, but the `onRate` closure only flipped `showPostRatingStep = true` *after* that `await` completed — a window where `displayScore != nil && !showPostRatingStep` briefly went true, swapping in `ratedBody` and unmounting `MorphingRateButton`, wiping its `.rated` phase; it then remounted fresh at `.idle` once `showPostRatingStep` finally flipped. Fixed by flipping `showPostRatingStep` synchronously, before the network `Task`.
>    - **Safe-area layout bug in `RateGaugeView` (drag-to-delete):** the trash icon rendered ~59pt *below* where it actually hit-tested — confirmed empirically by dragging directly onto the visible icon (nothing happened) vs. well above it, into the status-bar area (blur intensified, arc faded — primed). Cause: only the blur `Rectangle` had `.ignoresSafeArea()`; the outer `ZStack` didn't, so `trashTarget`'s `.position()` and `gaugeCanvas`'s default sizing were laid out within the safe-area-inset frame while the hit-test math (`isOverDeleteZone`) compares true global/window coordinates. Fixed by moving `.ignoresSafeArea()` to the whole `ZStack` — re-verified live, icon and hit-zone now align exactly.
> 1d. **Third follow-up, same day: the "Add a comment" row's disclosure chevron looked "weird" — should point right, then turn to point down, with clean motion.** It was swapping between two unrelated SF Symbols (`chevron.down`/`chevron.up`) — no shared glyph to interpolate, so it jumped instead of turning. First fix used a single `chevron.right` glyph rotating 90° (matching "Add to a Mix"'s static chevron) — **immediately corrected** once the user pointed out the two rows now looked identical despite meaning different things ("Add a comment" expands inline; "Add to a Mix" opens a separate modal sheet). Settled on `chevron.down` rotating 180° instead: still one glyph turning smoothly, but visually distinct at rest from "Add to a Mix"'s static right-pointing chevron, so the two affordances (inline-expand vs. navigates-to-modal) read differently again.
> 1e. **Fourth follow-up, same day: the expanding comment box was briefly visible *above* the "Add a comment" row during the reveal, overlapping it.** The box already had `.transition(.opacity.combined(with: .move(edge: .top)))` — `.move(edge: .top)` makes an inserted view start shifted *up* by its own height (enters by sliding *down* from above), which for a box sitting directly below that row meant it started out overlapping it on the way in. Dropped the `.move` component, left `.transition(.opacity)` — fades in at its actual position instead of sliding in from a displaced start.
> 1f. **Fifth follow-up, same day: user wanted stages 2→3→4 (drag flower → release → draft card) connected as one continuous motion** — the flower morphing into the *exact* badge at the *exact* spot it's already sitting, with the rest of the draft card growing in around that same instant, not a separate pop. Added a shared `@Namespace` (`ratingNamespace`) in `AlbumDetailView`, threaded as an optional `matchedGeometryNamespace` param into both `MorphingRateButton` (applied to the flower) and `FeedCard` (applied to its `ScoreBadge`) — both opt-in, nil everywhere else in the app, zero effect on any other caller. The flower and the eventual badge now share a `matchedGeometryEffect` id, so SwiftUI interpolates position *and* size between them directly. Two supporting fixes were needed for this to actually fire at the moment of release rather than one beat late: (1) a new view-local `optimisticScore` set synchronously inside the same `withAnimation` as the commit (deliberately *not* written through `viewModel.userScore` directly, since `setRating` captures its own "old" value for rollback-on-failure and pre-setting it there would corrupt that); (2) since the real post-shaped row (`myPost`) takes a network round trip to load, added a `myPost`-independent badge-only fallback (using the already-known local score) so the morph target exists instantly rather than waiting on that fetch — the real `FeedCard`'s badge picks up the same id seamlessly once `myPost` arrives. The draft card's own entrance changed from a flat `.opacity` fade to `.scale(scale: 0.94, anchor: .topTrailing).combined(with: .opacity)`, growing from the same corner the morph is happening in.
> 4. **Both phases, plus the 1b–1f refinements, fully re-verified live**, with real Supabase writes: rated a fresh album (KL7 by Kendrick Lamar, then WINGS by BTS after 1b, then OK Computer by Radiohead after 1c, then Kid A by Radiohead after 1d, then a Radiohead single by X-Dream after 1e/1f) → vivid idle button → small right-aligned flower → committed → **one connected draft card** (post preview, no heart/comment icons, divider, comment/mix/Done, right-pointing chevron matching Add to a Mix) → Done collapsed the appended step and the card became the normal complete post with its action bar restored. For 1f specifically: caught the transition mid-flight via a screenshot ~80ms after the drag-release tap — confirmed the badge rendering exactly at the flower's former position while the rest of the card (avatar, comment/mix/Done rows) faded in around it in the same motion, then confirmed a clean settled end state. Drag-to-delete tested on two already-rated albums — deleted, confirmed via rating-count drop, then **restored the accidentally-deleted real "Boyhood" 4.5 rating** (own data, not test data) by re-dragging to 4.5. Tracklist "+" buttons confirmed at the new consistent 30pt.
> 5. **Simulator window moved mid-session (VS Code took focus) — cost real time re-navigating before catching it.** Old tab-bar coordinates (derived from a % estimate of a full-window screenshot) kept "succeeding" by coincidentally landing on feed rows instead of erroring visibly, so the mistake wasn't obvious until deliberately bisecting the true tab-bar Y with a tight crop of just that region. Lesson for any future simulator automation in this app: after any window move/refocus, re-derive tap coordinates from a tight crop or `simctl io screenshot` cross-reference (as done for the Phase B trash-zone calibration earlier this session) rather than trusting a percentage estimate off a full, heavily-letterboxed window screenshot.
> 6. **Not committed.** Not done: `QuickAddRow`/`QuickAddSongRow` (`Main/QuickAddView.swift`) still have no delete capability at all — flagged as an explicit out-of-scope gap in the plan, not touched.

> **✅ SESSION CLOSE (2026-08-07, Mac, session 2) — User-reported slow tab/page loads + two false-negative UI bugs, all root-caused and fixed. COMMITTED + PUSHED (`95e561d`, `8621fb5`).**
> 1. **Perf: found and fixed serial-await waterfalls in the three highest-traffic load paths.** `AlbumSocial.loadCounts`/`loadMyLikesAndSaves` (`AlbumDetailView.swift`, hit on every album-page visit), `HomeViewModel.loadSocialData`/`loadMixShareSocialData` (`HomeView.swift`, gates Home's cold-launch first paint), and `ProfileViewModel.loadMixShareSocialData`/`loadSongRatingSocialData` (`ProfileView.swift`) each fired 2–5 independent Supabase queries **sequentially** instead of concurrently — a leftover gap in an otherwise already-heavily-optimized codebase (Charts/Discovery/most of Profile already use `async let` fan-out from prior sessions). All four converted to `async let`, matching the idiom already used elsewhere in the same files. Home's load path alone drops from ~8 sequential round-trips to ~2.
> 2. **Bug: Mixes subtab occasionally showed "No mixes yet" falsely.** Every account has a server-side-guaranteed default "Listen Later" mix (trigger in `20260620000001_mixes.sql`), so a genuinely empty result should be impossible — but `MixLibraryViewModel.load()` used `try? ... ?? []` on its primary fetch, so a transient network failure silently read as "zero mixes" and the `hasLoaded` guard then latched that false state until something else (creating a mix) triggered a reload. Fixed: fetch as `[Mix]?` (nil = real failure, not empty), retry once, only latch `hasLoaded` on success, and show a distinct "Couldn't load your mixes" + Retry state instead of the empty-state CTA on failure.
> 3. **Bug: Taste tab occasionally showed the "Rate N more to unlock" lock screen falsely.** Same root shape as #2 — `TasteViewModel.load()`'s album/song rating counts used `try? ... ?? 0`, so a flaky count query could report 0 for a user with hundreds of ratings, failing the `>= 25` unlock check. Fixed with the same pattern: optional fetch, one retry, distinct failed/retry state instead of falling through to the lock screen.
> 4. **Verified via `xcodebuild -scheme sillajuku -destination 'generic/platform=iOS Simulator' build` → BUILD SUCCEEDED after every change. Not walked through live in a simulator/device session** — worth a hands-on pass (cold launch timing feel, forcing a network blip mid-load on Mixes/Taste to confirm the retry states render) before considering this fully closed.

Fixed on both platforms by passing provider-specific query params (`prompt=select_account` for Google, `show_dialog=true` for Spotify) through `signInWithOAuth`/`linkIdentity` (iOS: `AuthViewModel.swift` + `ConnectedAccountsViewModel.swift`; web: `app/(auth)/login/page.tsx`) — GoTrue forwards arbitrary queryParams straight to the provider's own `/authorize` URL. Apple is unaffected (native `ASAuthorizationController` flow on iOS, no documented equivalent param on web) and left alone. `xcodebuild` + `tsc --noEmit` both clean.

> **✅ SESSION CLOSE (2026-07-28 → 08-03, Windows) — zero-release artists: hard-link tier bulk-written and safety-audited clean (260 artists, 3,099 groups); alias tier fully swept and reviewed, nothing written. Started from a user report that Skyminhyuk showed 0 releases; closes with Skyminhyuk fixed, verified, and the broader problem measured, partially recovered, and everything unrecovered clearly documented as to why.**
> 1. **Root cause: a MusicBrainz coverage gap, amplified by our own `area` lane — not a false match, not an ingest failure.** The `area` discovery lane (2026-07-17) queues every MB `country:KR`/`area:<city>` **artist entity**, and MB entities exist without releases — so **8,973 artists (13.3%) had zero releases; KR was 7,662/12,413 = 61.7%**. **SHIPPED + APPLIED:** migration `20260728000000` hides zero-release artists from search (`search_artists` requires ≥1 renderable release, primary UNION credited); `artists_with_releases(uuid[])` added for the typeahead. Deliberately did NOT touch `ingest_state` — the FRESHNESS lane's `claimDueFreshness` selects on `tracks_done`, so changing it would kill the self-heal path.
> 2. **`scripts/resolve-empty-artists.ts` — the recovery half.** Hard-link tier (MB URL rel → explicit streaming id, no name matching) is the default and considered safe for auto-write. An opt-in `--tier=alias` (MB-alias-corroborated iTunes name resolution) exists for the ~91% with no hard link, but its own development found **4 separate false-positive classes** (whitespace-deleting key, MB `sort-name` treated as an alias, CJK-only generic-name guard, romanized Korean given names) — it is **dry-run → human review → `--only-ids` write ONLY, never bulk-auto-write.**
> 3. **RESULT: 260 distinct artists recovered, 3,099 release groups** (1,365 itunes · 1,578 deezer · 156 spotify) across two bulk `--all --write` passes plus 3 individually-verified writes (Nawhij, Skyminhyuk, Briakitten). **Skyminhyuk itself: 21 groups via `itunes-alias`**, matched on MB's real alias 스카이민혁 — audited clean, idempotent, now findable in search with a populated page.
> 4. **A real data-integrity bug was found, root-caused, and fixed at the code level in all 3 ingest paths (iTunes/Deezer/Spotify) — read this before running the resolver again.** One artist ("Lino") had 63 release_groups left at `source=NULL`: a `recordings insert` timeout on ONE album threw out of the whole per-artist loop, aborting before the end-of-loop provenance-tag ever ran, so every earlier album that call had already written was left untagged and invisible to `reconcile-itunes-mb.ts`. Verified isolated to this one artist via a catalog-wide `source IS NULL` scan; repaired via direct SQL. **Fixed at the root**: every album's insert now runs in its own `try/catch` so one failure can't strand its siblings; the iTunes path also wraps its provenance-tag in `try/finally` as a second layer.
> 5. **⚠ The shared Spotify circuit breaker tripped TWICE this multi-day effort — own this before touching Spotify again.** First (2026-07-29): an unthrottled diagnostic script, fixed by adding `assertSpotifyCircuitClosed()` + a 250ms per-album sleep to the resolver. Second (2026-07-30): **the resolver itself**, despite that fix — ~630 artists into a bulk run, sustained volume across many artists exceeded what per-album throttling alone can prevent. Response: `spotifyPace()` — proactive backpressure (a minimum spacing on every Spotify call plus a forced cooldown every 40 calls), not just a reaction to 429s. **The breaker has since cleared naturally with time — but `spotifyPace()` itself is STILL UNVERIFIED LIVE**, since no session has run the resolver against Spotify since adding it. **Do not trust it at bulk scale — start the next Spotify run with `--limit` on a small batch and confirm zero 429s first.**
> 6. **Also found and fixed: a Spotify dry-run/write accuracy gap** (first live Spotify test, "Briakitten": dry-run said 6 groups, write produced 2 — not wrong data, a correct per-album artist-match guard was just running after the count instead of before it). Moved earlier so dry-run numbers mean what they say.
> 7. **Final catalog-wide safety sweep (not a sample — every one of the 3,099 groups written this effort): 0 orphan groups, 0 bad-canonical, 0 missing-provider-id, 0 null-source anywhere.** 11 groups (0.35%) have empty tracklists — same class as Lino's one incomplete release, matches the codebase's existing "lazy track hydration" convention, not corruption.
> 8. **`reconcile-itunes-mb.ts` widened to cover `itunes/deezer/spotify`** (was `itunes`-only — a real gap, since Deezer/Spotify-sourced artists here DO have MBIDs and ARE re-polled by FRESHNESS, unlike `mb-deezer-fallback`'s artists). A full dry pass found **0 already-duplicated rows**; 20 confident links applied.
> 9. **`--tier=alias` full sweep COMPLETED** (it turned out to be several real wall-clock hours, not the still-running state implied when this note was first written) — **8,712 candidates classified: 551 would-ingest / 4,019 groups, 7,584 alias-rejected, 340 provider-unavailable (itunes), 131 no-albums, 105 name-mismatch.**
> 10. **⚠ Spot-checking the 551 before handoff found a CONFIRMED false positive — a 5th distinct failure class in this tier.** "Christina" (52 groups) passed both the uniqueness and distinctiveness gates, but its one iTunes artistId turned out to itself be an Apple-side merge of unrelated people sharing that mononym — German Christmas harp music, Spanish pop, a Korean kids' song, Russian pop, spanning 1991–2026. **Fixed**: `resolveItunesByAlias` now fetches the resolved artist's discography once and rejects if its release-year span exceeds 25 years (verified: rejects Christina, doesn't over-reject Zack Knight/Rajvir Jawanda's genuinely coherent 11yr careers). Retroactively applied to the completed sweep (no re-run needed): **515 cleared / 3,361 groups, 36 flagged / 658 groups** (same class as Christina — some, e.g. Pepe Blanco, are plausibly real long careers, not confirmed bad).
> 11. **This guard is NOT a complete fix — say so plainly, don't overclaim.** A spot-check of the "cleared" 515 found a different-shaped red flag it doesn't catch: "DJ Silent" (12 groups, all dated 2026 but bilingual Spanish/English with eclectic, disconnected-feeling content) — a same-era variant of the identity-merge problem, not the decades-spanning one the new guard addresses. **`scripts/data/alias-tier-review-2026-07-31.json`** is the reviewable artifact (both buckets, full title lists, this caveat stated explicitly). **Nothing from the alias tier has been written.** Given 5 failure classes found across this tier's development and at least one more suspected-but-uncaught, treat even the "cleared" bucket as needing real human review — spot-check for coherence, not just a rubber stamp — before any `--only-ids --write`.
> 12. **NEXT SESSION, in order:** (a) small `--limit` test of `spotifyPace()` — confirm zero 429s before anything bigger; (b) if clean, `--all --write --resume` to catch the ~444 hard-link artists still sitting at `provider-unavailable` from the Spotify-blocked runs; (c) separately, human-review `scripts/data/alias-tier-review-2026-07-31.json` (551 candidates, 515 cleared / 36 flagged — read its own warning before trusting either bucket) and `--only-ids` write whatever's approved.
> 13. **Known, NOT fixed (flagged):** `app/sitemap.ts` still submits all artist ids to search engines including empty ones (thin-content SEO); a direct `/artist/<id>` URL for an empty artist still renders empty rather than a proper empty state; the resolver writes `release_groups.primary_artist_id` but no `release_group_artists` row (harmless — that table has zero consumers in `app/`/`lib/`/`components/`).

> **⚑ Vercel free-tier Fluid Active CPU exhausted (2026-07-30) — root-caused, partial fix shipped, user already upgraded to Pro.** User got Vercel's "100% of included Fluid Active CPU used, projects will be auto-paused" notice and asked whether to switch to AWS. **Root cause, not a platform problem:** `robots.ts` was relaxed from a blanket pre-launch `disallow: '/'` to default-allow as part of App Store launch prep, and `sitemap.ts` actively lists ~460k real URLs (414k albums + 49k artists, each a Supabase-backed SSR render) for crawlers to fetch — a cost the sitemap's own comment already flagged when it excluded songs (3.16M) for the same reason, but judged albums+artists "comfortably cheap." That assumption is what the actual bill just disproved. AWS would cost the same for the same per-page compute — switching platforms doesn't fix this.
> 1. **User already paid for Vercel Pro ($20/mo)** — confirmed as sensible regardless of root cause: an auto-pause risk while a build sits in App Store review is real downside, and Pro removes the hard pause in favor of headroom + pay-as-you-go. Not wasted money, but doesn't fix the underlying driver on its own.
> 2. **Shipped:** `app/robots.ts` now explicitly disallows known AI-training/answer-engine crawlers (GPTBot, ClaudeBot, CCBot, Google-Extended, Bytespider, PerplexityBot, etc.) — these are "well-behaved" bots that obey robots.txt (that's *why* blocking works) but send zero real traffic back, unlike Googlebot/Bingbot, which stay fully allowed. Zero SEO cost, real load reduction. `tsc --noEmit` clean.
> 3. **Not done, flagged as follow-ups (bigger or external-dependency changes, left for the user to prioritize):** (a) Search Console crawl-rate limiting — external, needs the user's GSC login, can't be done from here; (b) converting album/artist pages to true static generation with on-demand revalidation tied to actual data changes, instead of the current blanket `revalidate: 3600` — the real structural fix, but a bigger, riskier change across every catalog page that deserves its own pass rather than folding into this session.
> 4. **Worth watching:** whether the AI-crawler block alone is enough to keep future usage inside Pro's included allowance, or whether the bigger static-generation change becomes necessary once real traffic data comes in post-launch.

> **⚑ SESSION CLOSE (2026-07-30, Mac) — App Store rejected AGAIN (submission `16d56cd0`, build 1.0 (13), reviewed 2026-07-31 on iPad Air 11" M3): Guideline 2.1(a) App Completeness — "an error message was displayed when using Sign in with Apple." A real functional bug this time, not a policy point, and Apple gave no repro steps or error text. Fixed the strongest available hypothesis + closed a real diagnostic gap so a recurrence won't be another blind guess. Open items, priority order:**
> 1. **Note: the repo's tracked build number (12) didn't match what was actually submitted (13)** — the user must have bumped it out-of-band (Xcode's automatic versioning, or an uncommitted local change) before archiving. Bumped `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION` **12 → 14** this session (safely past the burned 13, not just +1) to get back in sync — worth checking whether "manage version automatically" is enabled in Xcode's target settings, since that would explain the drift and could cause it again.
> 2. **Root-cause hypothesis (not confirmed — Apple gave no error text or crash log): `AppleSignInHandler.presentationAnchor(for:)`'s naive `.first { $0.isKeyWindow } ?? UIWindow()` lookup.** **Corrected mid-session:** the app doesn't declare `UIApplicationSupportsMultipleScenes`, so it can never have more than one `UIWindowScene` of *itself* alive — Split View/Stage Manager with a *different* app doesn't add entries to *our* `connectedScenes` (separate processes, separate scene lists), so multi-app multitasking was never actually a way to reproduce this. The real, narrower risk: our single window can be transiently NOT flagged key during a scene activation-state transition, and the old fallback then handed `ASAuthorizationController` a disconnected, scene-less `UIWindow()` with nothing to present on — closer to a race condition than something a manual gesture can force on demand. Still the strongest available hypothesis (exact code path, matches the symptom), still unconfirmed. **Fixed** (`AuthViewModel.swift`): prefers the foreground-active scene's key window, falls back to any key window, then any window at all, and only returns a disconnected `UIWindow()` as the true last resort — a strict improvement regardless of the exact trigger, since it only ever prefers a real window over the old blank one.
> 3. **Also fixed while in this code, real regardless of root cause:** a Supabase `identity_already_exists` conflict (plausible given App Review reuses the same Apple ID across every resubmission) now shows a clear message instead of a raw backend error string — parity with how web already handles this exact error code. **And: every caught auth error (Apple, Spotify, Google) now reports to Sentry via `SentrySDK.capture(error:)`**, which it never did before — if this fix doesn't fully resolve the bug, the next occurrence leaves an actual stack trace instead of another guess-and-ship round.
> 4. **Base case confirmed working:** user signed a real Apple ID into a fresh iPad Air 11" M3 simulator (created this session to match the review device) and Sign in with Apple completed successfully, all the way to the real Home feed. The multi-scene edge case itself couldn't be forced deterministically — see the correction in point 2 — so there's no manual repro left to chase; ship the fix on its own merits.
> 5. **USER: archive + upload build 14** (Xcode Organizer), reply to App Review, resubmit.
> 6. Verified via `xcodebuild ... build` → BUILD SUCCEEDED after every change including the build-number bump and the mid-session correction.

> **✅ SESSION CLOSE (2026-07-24, Mac) — Home/album-page "already rated" flower bug fixed on iOS; Charts tab's real bottleneck root-caused, fixed, applied, and reverified live; Add tab sped up. COMMITTED + PUSHED (`ec6dcad`, `adf20c6`, `5e81c22`). Nothing left mid-flight.**
> 1. **Rated albums showed the drag-to-rate flower instead of their score, on Home's feed and the album page.** Root cause: `AlbumRateButton`'s cover-corner instance only reflects a score it's explicitly given, and neither surface ever passed the viewer's own rating — Home's feed badge shows the *post author's* score (it's an activity feed of other users' posts), not the viewer's; the album page's posts and even the viewer's own "Your Rating" card had the same gap. Fixed: `HomeViewModel` now fetches the viewer's own ratings alongside its existing likes/saves fetch (a `[UUID: Double]` map, since Home shows many releases); the album page uses a single `viewModel.userScore` since every post there is about the same release. `xcodebuild` clean.
> 2. **Also removed the redundant flower from the album page's hero cover** (per user request) — the "Your Rating" section (a full post once rated, a "Rate this Album"/"Add to Rankings" button otherwise) was already the page's one rating surface; the hero's own overlay was a second, now-inconsistent way to do the same thing.
> 3. **User reported tab-switching felt slow; investigated with real numbers before touching anything** (temporary timing probes + a screen-independent XCUITest harness driving the real installed app, both torn down after). Cold-launch tab times: Home 3.6s, Profile 2.4s (fine) vs. **Charts 8.7s, Add 9.1s, Taste 3.4s** (not fine — Charts/Add show a full-screen spinner with nothing progressive).
>    - **Charts' cause: a previously-shipped perf fix got silently reverted.** `20260703000005_song_charts_lateral_fix.sql` had already fixed the 3 song-chart RPCs (`get_charts_top_rated_songs` etc.) — the original `loc` CTE's `ORDER BY` spanned two joined tables, forcing Postgres to sort the *entire* `release_tracks ⋈ releases` catalog before picking one row per recording; the fix scoped that lookup to rated recordings only via a `LATERAL JOIN`. A **later** migration (`20260706000002_charts_release_type.sql`, adding an `album_release_type` column) redefined the same functions again but was written against the *pre-fix* version, reintroducing the slow structure. Measured: ~8.4s per song RPC vs ~0.4s for the album equivalents. **Fix `20260723000000_song_charts_lateral_fix_reapply.sql` re-applies the LATERAL structure** (keeping the newer columns) — **user applied it via the Supabase SQL editor; reverified live with a fresh timing pass: Charts' total load dropped 8.7s → 1.6s**, all 10 concurrent queries now landing between 0.4–1.6s. Done.
>    - **Add's cause (fixed): `resolveRecentlyPlayedIfNeeded()` ran serially after `reloadDiscoverySections()`** despite not depending on it at all (it only needs the Spotify/Apple Music recently-played lists, already loaded earlier). Folded into the same concurrent task group. **Verified live: 9.1s → 7.35s.**
>    - **Not fixed, flagged as a separate follow-up:** `/api/recommendations` (4.5s) and `/api/taste/profile` (2.8s) are both slow Next.js/Vercel routes — server-side compute or a DB query in TypeScript, not root-caused yet. Even with the above fixes, Add has a ~4.5s floor and Taste a ~3s floor until those are investigated separately.

> **✅ SESSION CLOSE (2026-07-23, Windows) — Web Instinct-mode parity on the rate buttons + album score gate + a class of modal/nav bugs, all browser-verified. COMMITTED + PUSHED.** User reported the web app didn't match iOS when in Instinct mode. Fixed, in layers:
> 1. **Cover rate buttons are mode-aware now.** `components/sj/AlbumRateButton.tsx` always rendered the drag-to-rate flower regardless of mode, so Instinct users could drag a *manual* score straight into `ratings.score`, bypassing the pairwise flow. In Instinct mode it now shows a **plus → check** button that opens `InstinctModal` (drag disabled), reactive to the session `profile.rating_mode` so flipping Settings swaps every cover live. The **Add tab is `/search`** (not `/quick-add` — that's a secondary page reachable from a banner); both had the same hardcoded-flower bug and both were made mode-aware (`InstinctAddButton`).
> 2. **Album page hides the Instinct score below 5 ranked** (`app/(main)/album/[id]/page.tsx`) — it rendered `eloToScore` unconditionally while the profile correctly gated on `INSTINCT_REVEAL_THRESHOLD`; added the count fetch + gate + histogram-highlight suppression.
> 3. **Three infra bugs behind the plus button, each browser-verified with the browse skill** (dev server is on **:3001**; :3000 is a *different* local app): (a) **`Modal` never portaled** → card-nested modals were trapped in the card's stacking context (badges painted over them) — now `createPortal`s to `document.body` at `z-[120]`; (b) **`nextjs-toploader`'s top progress bar false-started** — its `document` click listener fires for any click inside an `<a>`, and a React `onClick` can't stop a sibling listener on the same delegation node — new hook **`components/sj/useNavSafeClick.ts`** attaches a **native** click listener on the button so the click never bubbles to `document`; (c) **React portals bubble through the component tree, not the DOM tree**, so clicks inside the portaled sheet (incl. *close*) still reached the card's `<Link>` and navigated — fixed by `onClick={stopPropagation}` on `Modal`'s root. **Verified:** modal on top, opens without the gauge, and closes without jumping to the album page.
> - **Caveat:** the exact logged-in flow couldn't be driven headlessly (Supabase session is in localStorage behind OAuth-only login), so the *mechanisms* were verified via the shared `Modal`/toploader, not the live Instinct click. User confirmed the fix works.
> - **Left uncommitted (prior-session, out of scope for this push):** the KR cover backfill + video-track cleanup working changes (`display.ts`, `cleanup-video-tracks.ts`, `mb-client.ts`, `backfill-rg-covers-kr.ts`) — see the block below, still the active pipeline task.

User-facing slice = **3,662 KR-artist** groups (0 rated albums affected). Gotcha: the old `scripts/backfill-cover-art.ts` writes `releases.cover_url`, but every surface reads **`release_groups.cover_url`**. **New `scripts/backfill-rg-covers-kr.ts`** (defaults dry-run, `--write` to commit, resumable). **iTunes 403-throttles this box persistently** (cooldown + overnight didn't clear it) → reworked chain to **CAA(group) → Spotify → Last.fm → iTunes(fast-fail, auto-disables)** with match-validation guards. **Dry-run: 2,534/3,662 = 69%** (CAA 612 · Spotify 217 · Last.fm 1,705 · iTunes 0); accuracy spot-check = 0 mismatches, 2 covers visually confirmed. **`--write --reset` launched** (must include `--reset` — dry-run marked all rows processed). Additive/reversible (nulls only). **NEXT:** confirm final write count; a follow-up `--write --reset` pass idempotently catches Spotify-throttled stragglers. See SESSIONS.md 2026-07-22.

> **⚑ SESSION CLOSE (2026-07-23, Mac) — App Store build 11, Quick Add genre explorer, Profile-tab launch deadlock, then three more user-reported bugs (Explore/Following tabs, stale Instinct flowers, drag-rate precision). All COMMITTED + PUSHED (`c7a3554`, `d7f8c18`, `d779213`, `2acfe15`).**
> 1. **App Store build 11** — Guideline 5.1.1(iv): pre-permission screens now say "Continue" instead of "Allow X" (the real choice stays in the system dialog). Guideline 4: restored an optional "What's your name?" step, pre-filled when Sign in with Apple supplies a name, empty-with-placeholder otherwise, never gating Continue. **USER: archive + upload build 11, resubmit** if not already done.
> 2. **Quick Add gained a real empty-vs-seedless distinction** (a fresh account was shown the same "all caught up" message as an exhausted list) plus a full port of web's genre explorer (16 genres, `get_quick_add_genre_candidates` RPC, like-to-persist chip). **Settings → Connected Accounts gained "Connect Apple Music"** as its own row (device permission, not a linked identity) — previously there was no way to grant this after skipping it in onboarding.
> 3. **Rating-write error handling:** `AlbumQuickRate.saveManualScore`/`saveManualTrackScore` used `try?`, silently swallowing write failures while still posting `.ratingChanged` — every listener refreshed for a write that never happened. Now real `do/catch` + only posts on success; Quick Add rolls back its optimistic score on failure with a user-facing alert.
> 4. **Profile-tab "slow to load" → a real launch deadlock, not perf.** `MainTabView` eagerly loaded Home+Charts+Discovery+Quest together at launch; removing Charts/Discovery from that eager load (they already lazy-load on first tab visit) exposed a leftover gate — the whole `TabView` was hidden behind `homeVM.isLoading || chartsVM.isLoading || discoveryVM.isLoading`, and once Charts/Discovery stopped being preloaded their `isLoading` never flipped, so the tabs could never render to trigger their own load. Fixed by gating only on `homeVM.isLoading`. **Confirmed fixed against real repeated launches** — this was the one that mattered.
> 5. **Same day, three more direct user reports, each root-caused and fixed:**
>    - **Home's Explore/Following subtab switching was broken** — the bubble moved, the feed didn't. Root cause: `TabView(.page)`'s `selection` binding doesn't reliably respond to programmatic (button-driven) changes — a genuine, confirmed SwiftUI/platform limitation, not anything from this session (verified by testing the pre-session commit in a `git worktree`: bug reproduced identically). Three animation-related fix attempts failed before landing on the real one: replace `TabView(.page)` with `ScrollView(.horizontal) + LazyHStack + .scrollTargetBehavior(.paging) + .scrollPosition(id:)`, which supports genuine two-way state↔view binding. **Verified via a purpose-built XCUITest harness** (`xcodegen`-generated throwaway project driving the real installed app by bundle ID, screenshots pulled from the `.xcresult` bundle) — screen-independent, since the user was using the Mac for other things during testing.
>    - **Flower/drag-to-rate buttons kept appearing on Home after switching to Instinct mode** — `HomeViewModel`'s cached `rating_mode` was never invalidated. `SettingsView` now posts `.sjProfileUpdated` after saving the mode; `HomeView` listens and refetches.
>    - **The drag gauge ignored the user's rating-precision setting** — it snapped to a hardcoded 0.1 step regardless of whether the user had chosen half-star precision (the tap-to-open precise modal already respected it; only the drag didn't). Fixed on iOS, then **applied the same fix to web** per explicit instruction — `FlowerRateControl.distanceToScore` on both platforms now snaps to a `ratingStep` param threaded from `profile.manual_rating_step`, through `AlbumRateButton` and web's search/quick-add pages.

> 1. **Catalog-wide cleanup scan now running on Windows.** User investigation of a visible duplicate track on Aimer's *StarRingChild EP* found the real bug: `scripts/mb-client.ts`'s `parseMedia()` never checked MusicBrainz's `recording.video` flag, so bonus-DVD music videos (common on JP CD+DVD singles) were ingested into `release_tracks`/`recordings` as fake songs — sometimes with a title identical to the real track. **Fixed for future ingests** (`parseMedia()` now skips `video === true`; `total_tracks` also corrected to match). **New `scripts/cleanup-video-tracks.ts`** (dry-run by default, `--write` to delete, `--start-after <id>` to resume, `--only <id>` to spot-check) finds and removes the existing bad rows — verified correct against the real StarRingChild release (`--write --only`, confirmed the duplicate is gone and `total_tracks` corrected). A full catalog-wide **dry-run** was kicked off on the Mac (33,681 candidate releases — any release with a track on disc ≥2); the Mac never actually shut down so it kept running there uninterrupted to 21,300/33,681 (63%, 28,292 bad rows flagged) — **the user then also started it on Windows, so the Mac's copy was killed** to avoid a redundant parallel run. **The Windows run is now the sole authoritative one** — check its own progress there; this session has no visibility into it. **The catalog-wide `--write` has still NOT been run anywhere** — needs the user's explicit go-ahead after reviewing the complete dry-run report.
>    - **✅ Update 2026-08-12 — `--write` COMPLETE. 43,135 video rows deleted across 7,033 releases · 0 track_ratings touched · 2 releases unverified.** The **2,514 `gone` rows (530 releases) were deliberately NOT deleted** and are sitting in `apps/web/scripts/data/video-cleanup-report-2026-08-11.json` (11MB, `cls:"gone"`) for review — no ratings on any of them, and they're concentrated in a handful of huge box sets MB has re-tracked (Heino "Die große Sing mit Heino-Box" 124 rows, "Sing mit Heino" 115, Nirvana "Outcesticide Box Set" 109), which reads as MB re-editing rather than bad data on our side. Verified after: Aimer *Deep down* EP = 7 clean tracks, *Open α Door* = 32 (video disc gone). **Known cosmetic follow-up:** removing a middle video disc leaves the surviving discs numbered 1 and 3, so the album page's new disc headers render "Disc 1 / Disc 3" with a visible gap — decide whether to renumber sequentially for display.
>    - **Update 2026-08-11 (superseded by the line above): dry-run completed clean, then the tool was taught to classify.** Full dry-run: **33,680 checked · 7,030 releases · 45,588 flagged rows · 0 track_ratings at risk · only 2 unverified.** (The old "28,292 at 63%" figure is void — it predates the `getReleaseTracksOrNull` safety fix.) **Flagged rows are no longer deleted indiscriminately:** each is now labelled **`video`** (MB flags `recording.video=true` — the actual bug, deleted) or **`gone`** (absent from MB because a recording was merged or a release re-tracked — real audio that moved, **reported and left alone** unless `--include-gone`). Costs nothing: `--write` already re-fetches each release, so MB's video list was being discarded. Smoke-tested 150 releases → 194 `video` / 4 `gone`, i.e. ~2% ≈ **~900 real audio rows a blind write would have destroyed**. Classified rows persist to `apps/web/scripts/data/video-cleanup-report-2026-08-11.json` so a later pass over `gone` needn't redo the 10h scan. **Don't start the ingest pipeline while it runs** — shared MusicBrainz rate limit.

Only the Taste page (#6 graph rebuild + #7 report refresh) is left, and it is one session — a ready-to-paste prompt for it sits at the bottom of [`WEB_CHANGE_PROMPTS.md`](WEB_CHANGE_PROMPTS.md). Read that file first; it has the live status board and every "worth knowing" note.** Shipped in the final pass (pt 4): **#8 Profile list/table merge + filters**, **#13 Quick Add horizontal shelves**, **#14 Quick Add genre explorer** (migration `20260719000001_quick_add_genre_candidates.sql`, **applied 2026-07-19**), and **#12's rollout finished** — every hand-rolled `animate-pulse` in the app is now on the shared `Loading.tsx` primitives *except* `taste/page.tsx`, deliberately left for #6 to avoid converting a page that's about to be rewritten. Details of pt 4:
> - **#8 — the Rated tab's `list` and `table` modes were the same rows twice.** `table` was `hidden md:` *and* self-only, so nobody could use it on a phone or on someone else's profile, and each mode had its own sort model, so switching discarded your sort. Merged into one responsive grid (new `components/sj/ProfileRatedList.tsx`): the mobile list from `md` down, the sortable **Title · Artist · Type · Score · Date** columns from `md` up, both driven by **one** `sortCol`/`sortDesc` pair — the column headers and the `<select>` are two controls over the same state, so ordering survives a resize. Filters added and applied to the posts mode too: kind pills widened to **All · Albums · EPs · Singles · Songs**, plus a score-range disclosure (two 0–5 sliders, each clamping the other). CSV export survived the merge (it was table-only). `RatedTable` deleted; `ProfileExtras.tsx` is now just `SavedLibrary`. **⚠ The profile still only loads the most recent 60 album + 60 song ratings**, so filters and sorts operate on that window, not the whole library.
> - **#13 — Quick Add is horizontal shelves now, one per seed artist.** The flat prestige-ordered list buried every artist after the first, which is backwards for a page whose question is "do I remember this record". Each shelf is its own `get_quick_add_candidates` call with a **single-name** seed array — **no new SQL** — so the RPC's ordering and both exclusions apply per shelf, and shelves resolve independently. New `components/sj/CandidateRow.tsx` drives its paging arrows off **measured overflow** (`ResizeObserver` + `MutationObserver`), not item count, and hides rather than disables them at each end. "See more" opens a modal grid paging 24 at a time and keeps its own array so the page doesn't end up with a 100-card scroller. Infinite scroll now reveals more *shelves*, four artists at a time. **Songs mode deliberately keeps the flat list** (a shelf of near-identical text rows buys nothing).
> - **#14 — Quick Add can finally look outward.** Its seeds are the user's own history, so it structurally could never show them anything new. New migration `20260719000001_quick_add_genre_candidates.sql` (**applied + verified**) = `get_quick_add_candidates` with the seed predicate swapped for the charts' genre predicate (`_rg_primary_matches`) and **identical return columns**, which is what lets genre and artist shelves share one render path. The chip UI keeps **opening** a genre (a look) and **liking** it (a preference) as two separate taps, and likes persist to **`profiles.preferred_genres`** — the same column `lib/category-resolver.ts` already reads — so a like here also reshapes the home rows rather than being a throwaway local flag.
> - **#12 — rollout finished.** Converted: charts (×3), notifications, home `FeedSkeleton`, `post/[id]`, search, `song/[id]`, `HeaderMenus`' notification popover, `AlbumPeek`'s stats line + tracklist, `ProfileView` (page load, Mixes tab, `FollowListModal`), `ProfileExtras`' `SavedLibrary`, and Quick Add wholesale. `grep -rn "animate-pulse" app components` now returns only `Loading.tsx` and `taste/page.tsx`.
