> **⬜ SUPERSEDED by the block above — kept for the per-item detail. (2026-07-19, Windows, pts 1–3 — 12.5 of 18 at the time of writing.) The list + a live status board now lives in [`WEB_CHANGE_PROMPTS.md`](WEB_CHANGE_PROMPTS.md) — read that first, it says exactly where to pick up.** Done this session: **#2 gauge + color ramp**, **#12 loader component** (rollout only half-done), **#11 raw-ID flash**, **#18 "Go to charts"**, **#1 "Not interested" + the shared overflow menu**. **#1's migration `20260719000000_not_interested.sql` was applied to the hosted Supabase project on 2026-07-19** (via `scripts/db-exec.ts`) — table + RLS policy + index live, and `get_quick_add_candidates` now excludes dismissed albums server-side. Also done: **#3 right-click context menu**, **#17 add-to-mix → bookmark icon**, and **#5 + #16 (album-page flower rating — the clickable row and the cover button, done together so they agree)**, and in a second pass **#10 (mix page bug — see below, it was a live PostgREST error, not a UI problem)** and **#15 (grouped artist discography)**, and in a third pass **#4 (Stats tab rebuild)** and **#9 (@username over display name)**. **Next task is #6 + #7** (Taste graph rebuild + taste-report refresh — do them together, the refresh feeds the graph). Details:
> - **#4 — Profile > Stats rebuilt into `components/sj/ProfileStats.tsx`** (new file; `ProfileView.tsx` shed ~140 lines). The old tab had three different card treatments, a `max-w-lg` cap that ignored the page's own width, and — the real bug — **two undisclosed denominators**: the headline average was computed over manual scores only while the histogram underneath it included revealed instinct scores. One `scoreOf()` (applying the `INSTINCT_REVEAL_THRESHOLD` rule) now feeds the average, the histogram *and* the new per-artist averages, with the count stated on the card. Chart work follows the `dataviz` skill: percentage-based bar heights instead of raw px, bars coloured by #2's `spectrumRing(bucketScore)` (redundant with x-position, so nothing is lost to CVD), **selective** count labels (tallest bucket always, the rest on hover) instead of a number on every bar, a dashed average reference line, and the rating-mode tiles replaced by one legended split bar whose two segments now actually partition (instinct is elo-*only*). Empty and <10-rating states added. **Not touched: `RatingHistogram.tsx`** — despite the name it's the album page's community distribution, a different component. New i18n `sj.profile.{avgShort,statArtists,fromNScored,noScores,lowData}`.
> - **#9 — @username leads.** Audited every user-row surface: **most of the app was already correct** via `profileHandle()`. Only two were inverted — the profile header (which treated the *handle* as the fallback: `displayName || @handle`) and `FollowListModal`'s rows. Both now lead with `@username` and demote the display name to a secondary muted line, omitted when unset. `LikersModal` / `TrackLikersModal` / `CommentsModal` / `TrackCommentsModal` / `notifications.tsx` were already right. **The search page has no user results**, contrary to the task's guess. Presentation only — no query, column, or iOS payload changed.
> - **#10 — clicking a mix showed nothing because the query 500'd on every request.** `mix/[id]/page.tsx` embedded the author as `profiles(username, display_name)` off `mixes`, but `mix_likes` gives PostgREST a **second** `mixes ↔ profiles` relationship, so the embed was ambiguous and returned **`PGRST201` / 300 Multiple Choices for every mix, public or private**. The page destructured only `{ data }`, so a hard error arrived as `data: null` and fell into the "mix not found" branch — the `mix_items` query was correct all along and simply never ran. Fixed by pinning the FK (`profiles!mixes_user_id_fkey(...)`), **and the identical bug was found and fixed on the album page's "in public mixes" list** (`album/[id]/page.tsx:272`), which had been silently rendering empty. Both fixed queries were run against the hosted project with the anon key and now return rows. Hardening added while in there: both queries now capture `error`, log it, and render a retryable error state (reusing `sj.common.loadError` / `sj.common.retry`) instead of "not found"; the fetch is gated on `useSession().ready` (a separate latent bug — private mixes are owner-only, so a fetch racing session restore read as "not found" and never retried); and the bare `…` loader became a #12 skeleton matching the real layout. **⚠ Worth a sweep:** any embed crossing a likes/shares join table can carry this, and it's invisible wherever `error` is dropped.
> - **#15 — artist discography is sectioned now.** The Albums tab's flat list became a `<Discography>` component: sections **Albums · EPs · Singles · Compilations · Soundtracks · Other** (those are the actual distinct `release_group_type` values in the catalogue — single 195k / album 140k / compilation 82k / ep 39k / soundtrack 18k), each with a count, empty ones omitted, plus two pill controls — group **By type / All** and sort **Newest / Oldest / A–Z**. Grouping is a `useMemo` view over the already-fetched array, so the controls never refetch. Undated releases sort last in both directions. `typeLabelKey` gained `compilation`/`soundtrack` cases (with `sj.type.*` en+ko keys) — the old row hand-capitalised the raw column, which was never translated, so this fixes the label on the mix page too. **⚠ The `get_artist_release_groups` RPC still caps at 60 releases**, which grouping makes more visible than a flat list did.
> - **#2 — quick-rate gauge redesigned + new score color ramp.** `FlowerRateControl.tsx`: the flower now **stays put and stays visible** (gauge anchors to the button's centre, not the press point); the concentric rings/dashed cursor line are replaced by **one thin arc that fades along its length** at the current score's radius, angled at the cursor, over a **dotted horizontal baseline** with whole-star ticks; the **score reveals over the flower itself** (glyph fades out, number pops in with a spring) instead of a floating readout. Radii recalibrated (`OFFSET` 36 + `STEP` 34px/star, full sweep ≈206px) and the gauge now resolves to **0.1 increments**, not 0.5. `lib/sj/display.ts`: the HSL hue-sweep is gone — `spectrumFill`/`spectrumNumber`/`spectrumRing` now share **one OKLCh ramp at fixed lightness** (deep red → orange → yellow-green → teal → blue at 5.0), gamut-fitted by reducing chroma rather than clipping channels, so perceived brightness is constant; measured fill-vs-number contrast holds 5.6–6.3 across the whole range. New `spectrumColor(score, L, chromaScale)` export for charts. `ScoreBadge` and every badge picked up the new ramp automatically. **⚠ Two things to know:** `spectrumHue` now returns **OKLCh degrees, not CSS HSL degrees** (no caller relied on it, but don't pass it to `hsl()`); and **iOS `ScoreBadge.swift` still uses the old HSL sweep — a new parity gap**, deliberately untouched under the web-only rule.
> - **#12 — shared loading vocabulary (`components/sj/Loading.tsx`, new).** `FlowerSpinner` / `PageLoader` plus skeleton primitives `Skeleton` / `SkeletonLine` / `SkeletonBlock` / `SkeletonCover` / `SkeletonCard` / `SkeletonCardGrid` / `SkeletonRows`, all carrying `motion-reduce:animate-none`. **Only album + artist are converted so far** — a dozen pages still hand-roll `animate-pulse`; the exact remaining list is in `WEB_CHANGE_PROMPTS.md`.
> - **#11 — raw UUID no longer flashes on the artist page.** `artist/[id]/page.tsx` rendered `{name || rawId}` as its `<h1>`, so a UUID route painted the bare UUID until the `artists` row arrived; it's a skeleton line now (plus a skeleton avatar, and the tab body's bare `…` became `SkeletonRows`). The **album page never rendered its id** — its early-return skeleton already covered the gap; it was just rebuilt on the shared primitives.
> - **#18 — "Go to charts" removed** from under Trending on Home, along with the dead `sj.home.viewCharts` key in `en.ts`/`ko.ts`.
> - **#1 — "Not interested" shipped, plus the app's first reusable overflow (…) menu.** New table `not_interested(user_id, release_group_id, created_at)` (migration `apps/web/supabase/migrations/20260719000000_not_interested.sql`, owner-only RLS, **applied 2026-07-19**) and a `CREATE OR REPLACE` of `get_quick_add_candidates` adding a second `NOT EXISTS` so dismissed albums never come back — signature unchanged, no client change needed for it. New `components/sj/AlbumOverflowMenu.tsx` = a cover-overlay `…` button **plus an exported `OverflowMenuSurface`** positioned from a viewport point (not a button), which is what lets **#3's right-click menu reuse it at the cursor**; it portals to `document.body`, clamps to the viewport, and closes on outside-click/Escape/scroll/resize. Wired into **Quick Add** rows (bottom-right of the cover) and **Home explore** `FeedCard`s (one extra `MenuItem` in the existing inline menu). Both filter at render against a dismissed `Set` so Quick Add's pagination offset stays correct. The **Following feed is deliberately not filtered** — those are people you chose to follow, not recommendations. Helpers in `lib/sj/notInterested.ts` are best-effort (a missing table degrades to session-only filtering, never a dead feed). i18n `sj.notInterested.*` in en+ko; `done`/`undo` keys exist but no toast/undo UI was built.
> - **#3 — right-click menus, hung off the surfaces that already existed.** New `components/sj/ContextMenu.tsx` is a thin wrapper, not a second popover: `useContextMenu(items)` / `useContextMenuFor(build)` / `<ContextMenu>` all capture `e.clientX/clientY` and render #1's `OverflowMenuSurface` at the cursor, so viewport clamping and outside-click/Escape/scroll/resize dismissal come for free. It stops the event so the innermost wrapper wins (a track row inside a card shows the track menu, not both), and `wantsNativeMenu()` hands right-click back to the browser over inputs, textareas, contenteditable, and any live text selection. **Album covers got it globally by wiring `useAlbumContextMenu` into `AlbumPeek`** — that component already wraps every cover in the app, and using its existing root node means zero new DOM and zero per-page changes (Open in new tab / Rate… / Save to Mix / Not interested). Also wired: **artist links** via a new `ArtistLink.tsx` drop-in (album, song, search pages), **album track rows** (Open song in new tab / Rate…), and **mix rows** (Open in new tab / Remove from Mix, owner only). ⚠ `Modal` is plain `fixed` markup rather than a portal, so the context menu's dialogs are portalled to `document.body` — without that, a click inside the rating modal bubbles to the `<Link>` wrapping the cover and navigates away. New i18n group `sj.context.*` in en+ko.
> - **#17 — add-to-mix affordances now all use the bookmark icon.** `FeedCard` menu (`ListMusic` → `Bookmark`), `PostRatingOptions` (`PlusSquare` → `Bookmark`), the album page's header action (`ListPlus` → `Bookmark`), and `AlbumBookmarkButton`'s own "Add to another Mix" row. Behavior untouched — they all still open `MixPickerModal`. Icons that denote *a mix* rather than *adding to one* (the picker's rows, the profile Mixes tab, the album page's public-mixes list, home empty states) deliberately keep `ListMusic`.
> - **#5 + #16 — the album page's rating UI is all flowers now.** `InlineRatingEditor`'s five lucide `Star`s became `FlowerGlyph` marks with **real partial fill** (a clipped overlay copy, not the old whole-glyph `opacity-50` fake half-star), and each flower is split into `1 / step` invisible click targets — 0.5 by default, but derived from `profile.manual_rating_step`, so a 0.1 step gives tenths. Clicking routes through the existing `openEditor`/`update` path, so auto-save, undo, and remove are unchanged. On top of that, an **`AlbumRateButton` (the #2 drag gauge) now sits on the bottom-right of the hero cover** (hidden in instinct mode). Keeping the two in sync added two optional, backwards-compatible props to `AlbumRateButton`: `score` (controlled value, mirrored into internal state so a drag never lags a refetch) and `onScoreChange` (fired after the write lands → the page re-runs `loadRatings()`).

> **✅ iOS manual rating modal — slider replaced with draggable flowers (2026-07-16, Mac).** Per user spec: `ManualRatingSheet` (albums) and `TrackRatingSheet` (songs, same modal pattern) now use the Quick Add flower control instead of a `Slider` — drag/tap directly on five large (38pt) flowers; the numeric "X.X / 5" readout stays because at the 0.1 rating step a tenth of a flower isn't readable. `HalfStarRow` (QuickAddView.swift) generalized: new `step` param (0.5 default; manual sheet passes the profile's `ratingStep`, snapping + rounding to 2 decimals to kill 0.1-step float drift) and `onLiveChange` callback streaming the snapped value mid-drag so the numeric label updates live (commit still happens in `onEnded`). Quick Add call sites unchanged (defaults preserve old behavior). Verified on-sim via probe (0.1-step, 3.9 seed → "3.9 / 5" + 4th flower 90% filled); drag interaction itself not testable from CLI but the gesture is the same proven Quick Add code path.
>
> **✅ iOS Edit Comment gets its own modal + "list"→"Mix" terminology sweep (2026-07-16, Mac).** New `CommentEditSheet` (PostRatingOptionsView.swift): header + comment editor + Save only — deliberately no "Add to a Mix" row (that's its own menu entry now); song pages pass `trackTitle` so the header names the track with a "Song" chip. Both album/song Edit Comment call sites swapped from `PostRatingOptionsView` to it (that view stays for the post-rating flows). Terminology: user re-flagged that "list" isn't a product term — fixed the two stragglers ("Add to a list" → "Add to a Mix" in PostRatingOptionsView, "ratings, reviews, lists" → "mixes" in SettingsView's delete-account copy) **including ko translations in Localizable.xcstrings** (리스트에 추가→믹스에 추가 etc.; hand-edited — CLI builds don't sync the catalog, that's an Xcode IDE feature; also backfilled missing ko for "Add to Mix" and the new "Edit Comment"/"Your rating for this track will be removed." keys). Saved a persistent memory (`sillajuku-mix-terminology`) since this was the user's second correction. Verified both sheet variants on-sim via probe, reverted.

> **✅ iOS album + song pages (rated state) — rebuilt as post cards with ⋯ menus (2026-07-16, Mac, SUPERSEDES the same-day button-row/inline-comment version below).** User rethought the design: under YOUR RATING, show the user's own rating as the *regular post card*, with a triple-dot menu at the top right carrying all actions. Album page: `FeedCard` gained an optional `OwnRatingMenuActions` param (Share / Edit / Add to Mix / Edit Comment / Delete — Share keeps the Instagram-card `prepareShare` flow, Edit opens the mode-aware rating sheet, Edit Comment opens the `PostRatingOptionsView` sheet); `AlbumDetailViewModel` gained `myPost: FeedItem?` (own rating in post shape — `loadPosts` deliberately excludes it from `posts`) refreshed after re-rate/comment-edit/delete/instinct, and included in the social-counts batch. Song page: `ProfileSongPostCard` gained `SongOwnRatingMenuActions` (Edit / Edit Comment / Delete only — **songs have no share URL or mix membership anywhere in the app**, flagged to user) rendered as a top-trailing overlay (with conditional trailing padding so the score badge clears it); `SongDetailView` now fetches the full own `track_ratings` row (was score-only) into a `SongRatingRow` + like/comment counts + the profile-style elo-reveal gate count, and got track-level `updateTrackReviewText` (explicit-NULL encode) + `deleteTrackRating` + like toggle. **Side effect worth knowing: instinct-rated songs now show on the song page** (score-only fetch used to leave them looking unrated). Both pages verified on-sim via an env-driven RootView probe (`SIMCTL_CHILD_SJ_PROBE=album|song`), reverted after. Menu actions themselves (sheet opens) not tap-tested — all direct reuses of existing wiring.

> **✅ iOS Quick Add pill unreadable in dark mode, fixed (2026-07-16, Mac).** The Add tab's "Quick Add" capsule (`quickAddBanner`, `SearchView.swift`) hardcoded `.foregroundStyle(.white)` on an adaptive `Color.sjInk` background — `sjInk` flips near-white in dark mode, so the label vanished. Changed to `Color.sjCream` (flips dark in step), matching the existing idiom every other `sjInk`-background button already follows (`InviteView`, onboarding steps — checked, they were all already correct; Quick Add was the lone outlier). Verified in dark + light on-sim via a temporary default-tab-to-Add override, reverted after.

> **✅ iOS Home tab — liquid glass on Explore/Following/bell + status bar no longer a hard cutoff (2026-07-16, Mac).** Part 1: `feedTabButton` had no background at all — just bold/regular text weight to show the active tab. Added `.glassEffect(.regular, in: Capsule())` behind the active tab, shared `matchedGeometryEffect` id (`tabBubbleNamespace`) across both buttons so the bubble slides on tab change instead of popping. Part 2 (same session, user follow-up): the bell button got the same glass-circle treatment; separately, the whole top strip (status bar + header) was flat opaque `Color.sjCream` with a hard edge — nothing was blurred and nothing ever scrolled up into that zone since the ScrollView respected the safe area. Root fix: `feedContent` now `.ignoresSafeArea(edges: .top)` so scrolled cards can actually reach the physical screen top; a `topSafeAreaInset` captured once via a non-ignoring `GeometryReader` background keeps the header/feed's resting position pixel-identical to before on every device (no hardcoded safe-area constant). First attempt used `.safeAreaInset(edge: .top)` instead, which turned out to just reserve layout space without ever letting content scroll underneath — caught via an on-device screenshot showing the scrolled-away card had fully disappeared rather than peeking through blurred. **Blur tuning (same session, second user follow-up):** initial flat `.ultraThinMaterial` block behind the header read as "not gradual, too wide, too strong." Checked whether iOS 26's native `scrollEdgeEffectStyle(_:for:)` (the real Apple API for this) could do it for free — tested on-device and it produces **zero** effect behind a custom overlay header (only engages behind real system toolbar/tab-bar chrome), so no one-line fix exists here. Replaced the flat block with `.ultraThinMaterial` masked by a top-to-bottom `LinearGradient` (opaque at the true top, half-strength by 35% down, fully clear by 75% down) so it tapers instead of being a hard rectangle. `RankingsView`'s Albums/Songs toggle has an explicit code comment claiming "same style as Home Explore/Following" — still **not** touched (out of scope, user only asked about Home) and now visually inconsistent with it (no bubble, no top blur); worth asking the user if they want it matched.

> **↩️ iOS app icon / logo — attempted rebuild REVERTED, nothing changed on disk (2026-07-16, Mac).** User asked to make the app icon Liquid-Glass compatible. Misread the logo's halftone/CMYK-dot texture as file corruption (it renders as colorful dots at small sizes, and the "clean" `icon-flower.svg` next to it reinforced the wrong read) and rebuilt `AppIcon.png`/`AppIcon-dark.png`, all 7 `AlternateIcons/*.png`, and `logo-flower.svg` as a flat blue flower — user immediately corrected: **the dotted halftone flower IS the intentional brand logo.** Everything was reverted via `git checkout` (verified: only `HomeView.swift`/`SearchView.swift` remain modified). **Then re-prepped correctly and SHIPPED:** `apps/ios/LiquidGlassIconLayers/` got `foreground-flower-original.png` — the exact bytes of the original halftone flower extracted from `logo-flower.svg` (729×754 RGBA, real transparency, zero reprocessing) — the user did the Icon Composer session themselves (Icon Composer 1.6, the one bundled inside Xcode 26.6 — confirmed no separate download needed; the "2 beta" track pairs with Xcode 27 betas) and saved `appicon.icon`. Wired in by copying it to `apps/ios/sillajuku/AppIcon.icon` — capital name to match `ASSETCATALOG_COMPILER_APPICON_NAME = AppIcon`, and since the project uses a file-system-synchronized root group (objectVersion 77) no pbxproj edit was needed; Xcode 26 gives a same-named `.icon` precedence over the `.appiconset`. Verified compiled (`assetutil --info` shows `IconImageStack` + the flower layer in Assets.car) and on the simulator springboard in light + dark. **Notes:** user's Icon Composer design uses a **white** tile (not the old cream `#F5F0E8`) and keeps white in dark appearance (`darken` blend, no dark fill override) — their call, flag only if they ask why dark mode looks different from the old `AppIcon-dark.png`; the old `AppIcon.appiconset` still exists as fallback for contexts that don't support `.icon`; the 7 alternate icons (Settings → app icon picker) are separate flat PNGs, untouched and NOT Liquid Glass — making those layered too would need one Icon Composer session per variant. **All 7 alternates + switch-back-to-default runtime-tested** (user couldn't find the picker — it's invite-gated, `SettingsView.swift:84` requires `verifiedInviteCount >= 5`; tested instead via a temporary env-var-driven `setAlternateIconName` hook in `sillajukuApp.swift`, launched with `SIMCTL_CHILD_SJ_TEST_ICON=<name>`, springboard-screenshotted each, temp code reverted): every tile color correct with halftone flower, and Default correctly restores the `.icon`-based glass primary. Ships with the next App Store build (current submitted build 6 predates it). Useful facts confirmed along the way: Icon Composer lives at `/Applications/Xcode.app/Contents/Applications/Icon Composer.app`; `logo-flower.svg` is an `<image>`-wrapped base64 PNG (729×754), not true vector; alternate-icon PNGs are 120/180px with backgrounds matching `SettingsView.swift`'s `iconOptions` hexes; `qlmanage -t` works as an SVG rasterizer on this machine (no cairosvg/rsvg/inkscape).

Now surfaces far more often since the 2026-07-14 `search_release_groups` migration stopped filtering out compilations/soundtracks from search. > **✅ Quest timeline connector-line bug fixed, 2 tries (2026-07-15, Mac).** User: the line beneath a completed quest was turning blue even when a later quest wasn't done. First fix (`9ec41fd`) colored the line below item i by whether items 0...i were all done — still wrong, confirmed via a real on-device screenshot the user sent (Set Profile Picture through Connect Phone Number all done, but the line leading into the not-yet-done Invite a Friend was blue too). Off-by-one: that line connects item i to item i+1, so it needs the chain unbroken *through i+1*, not just through i (`ef76615`, `chainDone[index + 1]`). Verified by temporarily forcing `MainTabView`/`ProfileView` to launch straight into the exact same quest sheet, screenshotting via `xcrun simctl`, confirming the line was grey in the right spot, then reverting both temporary overrides before committing.

> **✅ Vercel "Fluid Active CPU" at 75% of free tier — root-caused + fixed one real bug (2026-07-15, Mac).** No Vercel API access from this machine, so diagnosed at the code level: `/api/recommendations` and `/api/taste/profile` do genuine in-Node CPU work (vector math, clustering — a deliberate CPU-for-DB-load tradeoff from the 2026-07-12 taste-vector rebuild), and both have server-side caches. But the Taste page called `/api/taste/profile?refresh=1` **unconditionally on every load** — `refresh=1` skips the cache read entirely, so every single page view forced a full recomputation regardless of how recently the same user last visited, unlike the sibling `/api/discovery`/`/api/recommendations` calls (built the same day) which correctly omit it. No comment or commit explained `refresh=1` as deliberate — read as a leftover dev flag. Removed it (`76bab2b`) so the page uses its existing 60s cache as designed. This won't zero out the cost — the underlying compute is a permanent, usage-proportional feature, not a one-time spike — but should meaningfully cut it. **Not yet confirmed against the actual Vercel dashboard** (no access from here) — worth checking usage trend over the next few days before deciding whether a TTL widen or a Pro upgrade is also needed.

> **✅ iOS recommendation-algorithm parity with web (2026-07-14, Mac).** iOS's Add tab ran its own older, separately-maintained recommendation logic while web went through a full taste-vector/genre-embedding rebuild (see the 2026-07-12 Windows entries below) that never reached iOS. Rather than reimplementing that algorithm in Swift, iOS now calls web's own `/api/discovery`, `/api/recommendations`, and `/api/taste/profile` routes directly (same Bearer-session-token pattern `SettingsView`'s `deleteAccount()` already used) — one algorithm, one source of truth. Popular is now prestige-ranked (was newest-first, mislabeled), a genuine New Releases row exists, Trending is bot-weighted, "For You"/"From Your Taste" use real taste-vector clustering, new "worlds" rows surface genre-cluster recommendations, and any artist rated ≤1.5★ is suppressed everywhere (iOS had none of this). Taste tab keeps its existing card-reel UI (scope decision) but now sources from the shared API instead of local computation. **Mid-implementation, a concurrent Windows push (Taste page v4, see below) removed the type/axes fields this work had just been built against** — caught via the merge, re-adapted to the new `charts.timeline`/`stats.fiveStars` shape, no breakage shipped. Also found + fixed along the way: Spotify/Apple "Recently Listened" was rendering raw external metadata with a lazy tap-to-resolve pattern (smaller cover, no add button, dead-end "not in catalog" alert on a miss) instead of resolving against the catalog up front like every other section — now resolves once at load time (batched, order-preserving) and renders through the same card component as everywhere else. That surfaced a real, high-impact catalog-search bug (below).

> **✅ FIXED (2026-07-14, Mac) — `search_release_groups` RPC silently excluded 41% of the catalog from every fuzzy search result.** Hardcoded `release_group_type IN ('album','ep')` since it was first written (flagged as a known gap in an earlier session, deferred) — singles are 169,564 of 413,812 release_groups, the single largest type, and Spotify/Apple Music singles rarely exact-match our stored titles byte-for-byte, so they fell through to this RPC and vanished even when genuinely in the catalog. Migration `20260714000000` drops the type filter entirely (also covers compilation/soundtrack) — applied live via SQL editor (this Mac still has no `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`), verified live. See the "FOR WINDOWS" list item 4 below, now marked done.

> **✅ Profile-page subtab swipe fixed (own profile) + added (other people's profile) (2026-07-13, Mac).** User reported swiping felt insensitive and sometimes opened whatever row was under the finger instead of switching tabs, on both profile screens. Root cause: `ProfileView`'s hand-rolled `DragGesture(minimumDistance: 15)` had a distance-only completion rule (33% of screen width, no velocity) — a real flick often falls short of both, so the gesture frequently never recognized and the touch fell through to the `NavigationLink` underneath (album/mix row) instead of switching tabs. `UserProfileView` (other people's profile) had **no swipe gesture at all** — tab switching there was tap-only. Extracted a fixed, shared `Components/SwipeableTabPager.swift` (used by both, so this can't drift out of sync again): `minimumDistance` 15→10, `onEnded` now honors a fast flick via `value.velocity` even short of the (also lowered, 25%) distance threshold, `.allowsHitTesting(false)` on page content during an active drag as defense-in-depth alongside `.highPriorityGesture`. Build succeeds; interactive re-verification on-device still needed (see aside below). See SESSIONS.md 2026-07-13 for the full root-cause trace.

> **ℹ️ Disk-usage question answered, no code change (2026-07-13, Mac).** User asked whether to stop the pipeline at 6GB/8GB (75%) Supabase disk. Real `pg_total_relation_size` breakdown (not the stale `measure-storage.ts`, which still checks pre-renovation columns) found **~3.1GB — over half the entire DB — is `release_groups`'s semantic-search embeddings** (1.89GB HNSW index + 1.29GB TOASTed vectors), not pipeline growth; genuinely pipeline-driven tables (`recordings`+`release_tracks`+`releases`) total only ~2.4GB. Not reclaimable bloat (autovacuum current, <6% dead tuples). **Recommendation given: don't stop the pipeline** — Supabase Pro autoscales past 8GB (billing, not read-only, unlike Free tier — worth confirming that's actually enabled on this project) and `ingest` was nearly drained anyway. Did suggest pausing the `discover` lane specifically (it had ~22k candidate artists still queued — the one lever still committing to *new* growth) while `ingest`/`freshness`/`gapfill` finish what's already committed. No action taken automatically — the user's call. See SESSIONS.md 2026-07-13.

See SESSIONS.md 2026-07-13.

> **✅ FIXED + SHIPPED (2026-07-13, Windows; pushed `b6207ac`, auto-deploys via Vercel) — web "infinite loading / hard-refresh sometimes works then breaks" was an auth-lock deadlock, not a cache problem.** The whole app gates on `useSession().ready`; it was being set *inside* the `onAuthStateChange` callback after an `await`ed `supabase.from('profiles')` query. auth-js 2.103.2 uses `navigator.locks` — the callback fires holding the session lock, the awaited query re-acquires the same lock → deadlock → `ready` never flips → app stuck on the loading skeleton. Intermittent because `getSession` and `INITIAL_SESSION` raced on lock timing. Fixed in `components/sj/SessionContext.tsx`: callback is now synchronous (`setUserId` only, no awaited Supabase call inside the lock); a separate `userId`-keyed effect fetches the profile off the lock and flips `ready`. Web-only, no migration, `tsc` clean. See SESSIONS.md 2026-07-13.
>
> **✅ NEW (2026-07-12, Windows) — taste vector + genre embeddings: web recommendations rebuilt, Taste page "worlds" cards shipped.** Genre-to-genre similarity now computed from our own catalog's tag co-occurrence (PPMI + truncated SVD over 307k tagged albums → 64-dim vectors for 1,015 tags; `npm run build:genre-embeddings`, artifact `lib/taste/genre-embeddings.json` + `genre_vectors` table) — researched external sources first: Last.fm tag.getSimilar is dead, ENAO frozen/mismatched vocabulary, so own-catalog co-occurrence won (validated: dream pop→shoegaze 0.83, k-pop nowhere near). New `user_taste_profiles` (migration `20260712000009` — renumbered from 000000 at merge, collided with Mac's genre_query_aliases; **applied** + backfilled 155/155 exact) maintained **incrementally by a ratings trigger** (verified insert/update/delete round-trip live). `/api/recommendations` rewritten (was orphaned + broken): already-rated albums never resurface, **artists rated ≤1.5★ fully suppressed**, candidates reranked in Node against multi-modal taste clusters (all vector math in-memory — zero extra Micro DB load), adventurousness-mixed, Redis 120s; web search page repointed with fallback. Taste page gained "Your Worlds" cluster cards via `/api/taste/profile` (en+ko). Verified live on real users (incl. the acid case: 16 low ratings on BTS/NewJeans → all blocked, pure hip-hop feed). **Part 2 (same day): Add page output restructured into taste-world sections** — `/api/recommendations` (cache `recs:v2`) now returns `fromYourTaste` + `forYou` + `worlds[]` (one cluster-pure "Because you love {X}" row per taste world) + `blockedArtists` (client applies ≤1.5★ suppression to Popular/Trending too, which previously only filtered rated ids); verified e2e, 0 leaks / 0 cross-section dupes. **Part 3 (same day): Popular/Trending fixed + Taste page → one-page MBTI-style report.** Trending was 99.2% bot-driven (7,653/7,712 of 30d ratings are bots, no filter) and "Popular" was just newest-first — new globally-cached **`/api/discovery`** route: prestige-ranked Popular, honest New Releases row, real-weighted Trending (1 real = 20 bot); verified live (trending now leads with real-user picks). Taste page rebuilt from the snap reel into a single scrollable report in the profile design system: 4-axis taste type (Range/Era/Reach/Scoring → 4-letter code + composable name, e.g. "The Open-Hearted Wave Rider", 16 types, en+ko), axis spectrums, worlds, stat tiles, #1 album, community standings, disliked chips — all from one `/api/taste/profile` (v2) payload. **Part 4 (same day): primary-genre weighting + interpretive report.** Tag arrays turned out to be **alphabetical** (MB API order; vote counts were discarded at ingest) so position weighting was replaced by **primary-genre weighting** (scene-first precedence: primary tag 1.0, co-tags 0.5) across TS + SQL trigger (migration `20260712000010`, **applied**, round-trip verified; ⚠ family table now lives in 3 synced copies: `lib/taste/primaryGenre.ts`, `_primary_genre_tag()` SQL, `backfill-primary-genre.ts`). `mb-client.ts` now sorts future ingests by MB vote count (**dormant until next pipeline restart**). Taste page upgraded to a full graphical analysis report: composition bar, per-world sub-genre breakdowns, dials with ±1σ bands (era/scoring) and rule-based interpretation sentences (en+ko), `/api/taste/profile` v3. **Part 5 (same day): RYM-style sub-genre enrichment + composite blob scoring.** New `npm run enrich:subgenres` (Last.fm album.getTopTags, MB-canon-filtered, importance-ordered, never removes tags) added **1,047 sub-genre tags across ~770 rated/prestige albums**; embeddings rebuilt (visibly better neighbors), taste profiles re-backfilled, and `rg_primary_genre` refreshed (its trigger only fires on rating INSERT — appended tags would otherwise never update chart classification). Candidates are now scored as **genre-vector ⊕ era ⊕ scene blobs** (0.65/0.20/0.15) against each taste world's own era/scene profile (`lib/taste/albumVector.ts`, `clusterProfiles()`, recs `recs:v4`) — verified e2e: a 90s-hip-hop world recommends 1989–2001 golden-age rap, not modern trap; taste worlds show "mostly 2010s · Korean scene" chips. Re-run enrich+embeddings+backfill occasionally as ratings grow. **iOS not touched (needs Mac build): adopt `/api/recommendations`'s section contract, `/api/discovery`, and the report layout next.** Audio-features API research (Task 3): **don't integrate now** — Spotify's endpoint is dead for new apps; AcousticBrainz 2022 dump (free, MBID-keyed) is the best future option when DJ-sequencing becomes real. See SESSIONS.md 2026-07-12.

> **✅ Genre-aware search shipped (2026-07-12, Mac) — "korean hiphop"/"kpop" and similar genre phrases now return real genre-tagged albums, not just literal title matches.** Search was purely lexical before this (confirmed: no LLM anywhere in the codebase, no genre-array matching in either search RPC — the web's embedding boost only re-ranks rows that already passed a lexical filter, never expands the candidate set). New `genre_query_aliases` table (seeded from the two genre taxonomies already in the codebase — `genre-categories.ts` and `_compute_primary_genre`'s family table, both previously dead code) blends genre-tagged albums into `search_release_groups`'s existing ranked results — same signature, **zero iOS/web client changes**, live server-side only. Took 6 live-tested fixes past the initial design: (1) a correlated per-row genre check timed out (57014) — restructured as an indexed JOIN + new trigram index (`_genres_text()`, an explicit `IMMUTABLE` wrapper, same class of fix `normalize_text()` needed); (2) still returned zero results once fast — turned out Korean hip-hop releases carry no nationality-specific genre tag at all (Simon Dominic = plain `["hip hop"]`), fixed by gating Korean/Japanese-scene phrases on `artists.country` in addition to genre; (3) a second timeout from short/generic filter substrings (`'rap'`) even with the trigram index — fixed with a `LATERAL` + per-filter `ORDER BY ... LIMIT`; (4) "kpop" specifically still timed out — K-pop's match set is too large for an ordered per-filter scan to early-exit, fixed by dropping the inner ORDER BY; (5) "kpop" then returned noise ("Pinkpop 2010") instead of timing out — the flat genre score lost to coincidental fuzzy trigram matches, fixed by raising it to 1200; (6) dropping the inner ORDER BY then cost "korean hiphop" its quality (arbitrary unordered sampling missed the best rows) — fixed by widening the inner sample so the outer prestige-aware sort has good candidates to choose from. Verified live: "korean hiphop" → Changmo/The Quiett/PENOMECO; "kpop" → ATEEZ/Zion.T/NCT 127/TWICE/Kep1er; ordinary searches unchanged and fast (~0.4-0.5s) at every step. **Known, accepted limitations** (catalog data, not search bugs): K-pop groups with hip-hop co-tags bleed into Korean hip-hop results (same issue the `primary_genre` chart system was built to solve, but that only covers ~3,644 chart-eligible rows); a meaningful chunk of the Korean indie catalog (검정치마, most of dress) has `genres = NULL` entirely, unfindable by genre no matter how the query logic works. See SESSIONS.md 2026-07-12 for the full 6-round trace.

> **✅ Add tab "For You" fixed — was completely static (never refreshed, zero randomization), and had no real recommendation logic behind it (2026-07-10, Mac).** Confirmed via code read: the seed pool counted every rating equally regardless of score (a 1-star rating seeded recommendations same as a 5-star one) and could only ever resurface artists already in the user's own history. Now blends that "exploit" slice (score-filtered ≥3.5, capped 3-per-artist) with a new content-based "explore" slice powered by a new RPC (`get_taste_similar_releases`, migration `20260710000000`, **applied**) using `release_groups.embedding` (Jina v3, HNSW-indexed) — the first real use of that embedding column for anything other than text search. Took 3 rounds of live-tested fixes to get right: (1) raw embedding distance is dominated by title-string tokens, not genre, since it's a text embedding not an audio one — gated on genre-array overlap; (2) a single shared *broad* genre tag let title-text noise back in — tightened to require 2+ overlapping genres; (3) HNSW's filtered-search recall collapses under a tight filter unless `ef_search` is widened — done via `set_config()` inside a `plpgsql` function body (the function-level `SET` clause form gets "permission denied" on Supabase's SQL-editor role). Also found a real performance cliff: cost scales with seed count, not per-seed limit — 5 seeds reliably hit the statement timeout; settled on 3 (deduped by artist). Refresh path added too: `.refreshable` pull-to-refresh (mirrors `HomeView`'s existing pattern) plus shuffling, so the section no longer looks frozen for the entire app session. Verified live: real quality improvement (Simon Dominic → La Fouine/DMX/D Savage, genuinely hip-hop; a real account's NewJeans/ATEEZ seeds → ITZY/BTS/BIGBANG among the results) and genuinely different content across two full relaunches. **One transient empty-section observed** during a burst of rapid manual RPC testing against the same DB, not reproduced on a clean run — flagged, not chased further, since the code already degrades gracefully if just the explore call fails. See SESSIONS.md 2026-07-10 for the full three-round trace.

> **✅ NEW (2026-07-10, Windows) — coverage verification harness + romanization-aware matching fix. `npm run verify:coverage` (READ-ONLY) measures how well the catalog matches real iTunes/Spotify discographies (the same source users' connected data comes from), classifying every miss as catalog-gap vs match-bug. Baseline found the biggest bug: `resolveArtist`/`fetchRelease`'s client gate only checked native `name`/`name_native`, so a CJK-named artist matched via a romanized ALIAS (혁오 via "HYUKOH") was thrown away — broke Spotify/Apple matching for most of the target audience. Fixed (`20260710000001` applied live: search_artists returns `aliases[]` + alias-exact ranking; search_release_groups returns `primary_artist_id`; iOS gates + harness consume them). Artist resolution 79.5% → 95.5%, mainstream floor unchanged (no false positives). Then edition/version decoration stripping (`20260710000002`, query-side in `search_release_groups`, no client change) took album+EP match **52.1% → 58.8%** (match-bugs 32 → 18). And the durable stale-name self-heal is done (`mb-ingest.ts` now refreshes `artists.name` from MB's current primary on re-poll, guarded — no more silent revert of the YANGHONGWON fix). ⚠️ iOS unbuilt — needs a Mac build. Full trace in SESSIONS.md 2026-07-10. Remaining workstreams: (a) accent folding (Blase vs BLASÉ — needs `unaccent` in normalize_text + a GIN reindex window); (b) singles/compilations behind the album/ep type filter + K-pop wrapper titles; (c) real coverage gaps (JP ~34%) → `catalogGapArtists` MBIDs in `scripts/output/coverage-*.json` are the goal-directed seed list for the pipeline (#3, not yet run). > **⚑ FOR WINDOWS — 5 systemic pipeline gaps found via one-off Mac bug reports (2026-07-09/12). Each was only fixed for the single reported row — none of these have been audited catalog-wide. Treat the two entries below (the YANGHONGWON title/cover/name fixes) as *symptoms*, not the actual fix; the real fix for each is a script/design decision on this list:**
>
> 1. **MB release-level titles can differ from release-group titles, and we only ever capture the group-level one.** `mb-ingest.ts` writes `release_groups.title` from the release-*group*'s title only; when the underlying MB *release* has a different marketed title (confirmed for YANGHONGWON's "오보에" vs. "3 STEPS FORWARD, 2 STEPS BACK"), Spotify/Deezer-sourced titles can never exact- or fuzzy-match our row, and the album silently looks "not in catalog" from any external-source lookup. Scope is **unknown** — found by one user report, never audited. Needs: (a) a script that walks a sample of `release_groups` (start with recently-ingested or high-prestige ones), fetches each MB release-group `?inc=releases`, and counts how often the release title disagrees with the group title; (b) a durable storage decision — **don't just overwrite `title`** like the one-off fix did, since `mb-ingest.ts`'s upsert (keyed by `mb_release_group_id`) rewrites `title` from the release-group on every freshness re-poll and would silently revert a raw overwrite. Needs a real column (e.g. `marketed_title`/alias) that ingest populates and preserves going forward, plus a decision on whether iOS/web search should prefer it.
> 2. **No cross-source QC on Cover Art Archive images — a wrong (but community-"approved") image can ship as a release's canonical cover with nothing to catch it.** Confirmed one case (YANGHONGWON/오보에) where CAA's only front-cover image was a completely unrelated picture. CAA is ~224.5k of our ~295k covers currently. Needs: a spot-check/QC script that cross-references a sample of CAA covers against Deezer/iTunes for the same release (match by artist + title + track count, or ISRC if available) and flags disagreements for review, rather than trusting CAA's single "approved" image unconditionally. Even a low-cost sampled audit (start with prestige-pool artists, since those are the most user-visible) would be worth doing before assuming CAA-sourced covers are reliable.
> 3. **An artist's canonical `artists.name` can go stale relative to MB's own current primary name, and nothing re-checks it.** `artists.name` was `"Young B"` (YANGHONGWON's retired stage name) even though MB's own primary name for the same artist has been `"YANGHONGWON"` for a while — this **fully broke** `search_artists()` for his current name (zero results pre-fix), not just a cosmetic display issue. Root cause is upstream of this week — some earlier ingest path (likely a credit-string-driven artist creation, not a direct artist-page ingest) captured a name snapshot and never revisited it. **Before building a backfill, first confirm how (or whether) `artists` rows are durably linked to their MB artist MBID** — nothing in the columns inspected from the Mac session (`id, name, name_native, name_phonetic_ko, disambiguation, country, ingest_priority, next_check_at, …`) looked like a stored MB artist UUID separate from our own `id`, and our `id` is *not* the MB MBID (confirmed: they're different UUIDs for this artist). Without a stable join key, a systematic "re-verify every artist's name against MB" pass would have to re-search MB by name for every row, which is both slow and exactly the kind of lookup that's already shown to be unreliable (name drift is the whole problem). Worth checking `mb-ingest.ts`'s artist-insert paths (~L212, ~L286) for whatever field *does* carry the MB link today.
> 4. **✅ FIXED (2026-07-14, Mac).** `search_release_groups` RPC hardcoded `release_group_type IN ('album','ep')`, silently excluding compilations and singles from every fuzzy search result — singles alone are 169,564 of 413,812 release_groups (41% of the whole catalog, the single largest type). Directly broke iOS's new Recently Listened catalog-matching (see below). Migration `20260714000000` drops the type filter entirely (not just adding 'single' — also covers compilation/soundtrack); existing prestige_score-weighted scoring already keeps low-prestige singles from crowding out an artist's real albums on a broad search. **Applied live via SQL editor** (this Mac still has no `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`, see the sync-needed note above), verified live: a loose "Free" query that previously returned zero non-album/EP rows now returns singles/compilations correctly (e.g. SUPER JUNIOR's "Sexy, Free & Single" single, previously invisible).
> 5. **✅ MOSTLY ADDRESSED (2026-07-12, Windows). Korean artists with no Hangul name anywhere → unfindable by native-script search.** **True gap measured: 56** `country='KR'` artists (not the 352 `name_native IS NULL` headline — most of those already carry Hangul in `name`), of which 53 have releases. Script: `scripts/backfill-korean-native-names.ts` (`npm run backfill:korean-names`, dry-run default). **Source pivot:** MusicBrainz was tried first and rejected — these artists genuinely have NO Hangul alias in MB (verified SHINHWA/EVERGLOW/CRAVITY: only romanized aliases, or none), so the handoff's "MB pages often carry a Korean alias" assumption doesn't hold here. **Korean Wikipedia's `ko` langlink does** (Shinhwa → 신화), found via `opensearch` (case-tolerant; full-text search returns noise + misses stylized all-caps). Three guards (title-exact · Korean-musician-category · Hangul) so a generic-name collision ("Seven"/"Method") can't mis-map. **Written to `name_phonetic_ko`, NOT `name_native`** — name_native drives display (would wrongly show "에버글로우" for Everglow); phonetic_ko is search-only and matched by `search_artists`, so it fixes findability without changing display. **Filled 6** (SHINHWA→신화, EVERGLOW→에버글로우, Seori→서리, MYNAME→마이네임, SUPER JUNIOR-K.R.Y., Asian Glow — all verified live: `search_artists('신화')` → SHINHWA). The other 50 correctly stay null: brand/stylized names with no distinct Korean name (SMTOWN, CRAVITY's ko-article is literally "CRAVITY") or Deezer-only artists absent from Wikipedia (SYSTEM SEOUL) — those need another source (their own Korean name is genuinely not on Wikipedia). Re-runnable as the catalog grows. **Original report:** "시스템서울" (SYSTEM SEOUL's actual Korean name) returned zero results even though searching "System Seoul" finds the artist fine — confirmed `name_native IS NULL` and zero `artist_aliases` rows for that artist. **Scope, measured, not guessed:** 352 of 743 `country = 'KR'` artists (47%) have `name_native IS NULL` — but that headline number overcounts the real problem, since many of those already store the Korean spelling directly in the `name` field itself (e.g. 나훈아, 잔나비, 우원재 — already findable, since `search_artists` matches `name` too). The *actually* broken subset is specifically artists whose `name` is Latin/romanized-only with **no** Hangul representation anywhere (SYSTEM SEOUL, Suzy confirmed as two real examples from a small sample) — true count unknown without a script that checks whether `name` itself contains Hangul before flagging `name_native IS NULL` as a real gap. Needs: a script that (a) filters `country = 'KR'` artists to those whose `name` has no Hangul characters AND `name_native IS NULL` AND no matching `artist_aliases` row, to get a real count; (b) a backfill source — MusicBrainz artist pages often carry a Korean-script alias/sort-name even when the primary `name` is romanized, worth checking before reaching for Last.fm/Deezer.
>
> None of the five above are "fix once and done" — they're catalog-wide risk patterns first surfaced by single user reports. See the two SESSIONS.md/README entries directly below for the specific one-row fixes that prompted this list.
>
> **✅ Data fix — "3 STEPS FORWARD, 2 STEPS BACK" (YANGHONGWON) now findable + correct cover (2026-07-09, Mac).** Was a real album already in the catalog (not a pipeline gap) but stored under its MusicBrainz release-*group* title ("오보에", Korean) while Spotify/the user know it by the underlying MB *release*'s own different title — our ingest only ever captures the group-level title, so the English name had no way to match. Also had a wrong front cover — confirmed CAA's only (community-approved) image for the correct release MBID just doesn't match the real artwork; replaced with Deezer's cover (verified identical to Spotify's own art). Fixed as a direct one-row data correction: `title` → the English name (`native_title` untouched, so in-app display still correctly shows "오보에" via the existing Hangul-preference logic), `cover_url` → the correct Deezer image. **Caveat:** `mb-ingest.ts` upserts `title` from the release-group on every freshness re-poll — if this artist is ever re-polled (pipeline currently paused), this row's title would silently revert. A durable fix needs the ingest to separately capture release-level titles; flagged, not built this session. **Also found + fixed in the same pass (user tip: "Young B is YANGHONGWON's old name"):** the artist's own `artists.name` was stuck on his retired stage name "Young B" even though MusicBrainz/Spotify/Deezer all now file him under "YANGHONGWON" — `search_artists('YANGHONGWON')` returned zero rows before the fix, meaning name-based artist search/resolution was fully broken for his current name. Fixed (`artists.name` → "YANGHONGWON"); this one's durable since artist rows are only ever `.insert()`-ed, never re-upserted by name. Verified live: artist page now correctly shows "YANGHONGWON" with all 10 releases unified (both his old- and new-name-credited albums). See SESSIONS.md 2026-07-09 for the full trace.
>
> **✅ Fixed — album↔artist↔song navigation was double-pushing (tapping through re-showed the previous screen on top), and the Artist page's Songs tab was completely inert (2026-07-09, Mac).** Two real, separate root causes: (1) `AlbumDetailView`/`ArtistPageView` each additionally declared their own `navigationDestination(for:)` for a type already declared at their hosting stack's root — SwiftUI doesn't support two registrations for the same type in one stack; normalized so every hosting stack (Home/Rankings/Profile ×2/Search + the Home "add rating" sheet) declares `Release` + `ArtistDestination` once at its own root, and the two leaf views declare neither. (2) The actual double-push mechanism: `SearchView`'s item-based nav bindings (`recentlyPlayedNavTarget`/`spotifyArtistNavTarget`, used for Spotify rows needing an async lookup) were set once and never reset to `nil`, so a later push from within their own destination spuriously re-presented the stale binding on top — fixed by resetting each to `nil` in `.onDisappear`, applied everywhere this shape exists (`AlbumDetailView`'s `selectedSong` and the new `ArtistPageView.songNavTarget` too). Also built out the Songs tab from scratch — rows had no tap handler and no add/rate indicator at all; now navigate to a real `SongDetailView` with correct track position and per-user score, matching the Albums tab's existing row convention. Verified live via idb through all 4 hops (album→artist→album→song→artist again). See SESSIONS.md 2026-07-09 for the full trace.
>
> **✅ Fixed — Add tab's Spotify-suggested album/artist rows were mis-navigating ("not in catalog" / empty or wrong artist page) (2026-07-09, Mac).** Root cause: Spotify's title/artist/name strings rarely match the catalog's stored strings exactly, and the old lookup paths had no tolerance for that. Fixed via the same fuzzy `search_release_groups`/`search_artists` RPCs the main search bar uses, gated by an artist/name-overlap confidence check (no blind top-result fallback — confirmed live that an ungated fallback returns confidently wrong results, e.g. "Father EP" by Masta Wu fuzzy-matching to Vampire Weekend, and `search_artists("Masta Wu")` top-hitting the unrelated "Masta Killa"). Also fixed a narrower bug where a real catalog album ("USB" by Fred again..) still 404'd because `search_release_groups` hardcodes `release_group_type IN ('album','ep')`, excluding its `'compilation'` row — now tries an unrestricted exact match first. Verified live: USB → correct album page, "사이먼 도미닉" → correct Simon Dominic page (native-name match), "dress" → correct dress artist page (not a fuzzy false-positive). **Known gap, not yet fixed:** Apple Music-suggested *artist* rows still use the old ungated pattern and would show the same symptom — out of scope since the user's report named Spotify rows specifically. See SESSIONS.md 2026-07-09 for the full three-bug trace.
>
> **⚑ PRODUCT DECISION (2026-07-09) — no post-onboarding "connect"/"reconnect" prompts for Spotify or Apple Music, for either service.** Everything gets wired up once at signup/onboarding; the user is never prompted again afterward. Both the Add tab's Spotify reconnect banner (pre-existing) and a since-reverted Apple Music connect banner have been removed on this basis — **don't re-add either without checking this decision first.** Spotify's removal is also now provably safe, not just aesthetic: with the taste-refresh pipeline in place, any account that's connected once keeps non-empty DB data indefinitely, so the banner's old trigger condition (`needsSpotifyReconnect`) is practically unreachable now. `load()`'s invisible retry-on-tab-revisit fix for Apple Music (no UI, just silently notices if access was granted some other way) was kept — that's not a prompt, just quietly not missing a state change. See SESSIONS.md 2026-07-09 for both entries.
>
> **✅ Fixed — Add tab's Spotify "Your Top Artists"/"Recently Listened" rows were never rendering for any account that linked Spotify after signup (2026-07-09, Mac).** Root cause was independent of the refresh pipeline below: `SearchView.swift`'s `loadSpotify()` checked `appMetadata["provider"]` (the account's *original* signup method — e.g. "email") instead of `appMetadata["providers"]` (the full linked-identity array), so it silently wiped its own Spotify cache and bailed out on every load for any multi-identity account, regardless of Spotify being genuinely linked or how fresh the underlying data was. Verified live: real data now renders correctly after a fresh app launch. See SESSIONS.md 2026-07-09 for the full root-cause trace.

> **⚑ ACTION NEEDED — add `CRON_SECRET` to the Vercel project's environment variables (2026-07-09, Mac).** New Spotify taste-data refresh pipeline (see SESSIONS.md 2026-07-09) needs it there specifically — Vercel only auto-attaches the cron job's `Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET` header once the var is set on the project itself, not just in `.env.local`. Without this, `/api/cron/refresh-spotify-taste` will 401 against itself on its first scheduled run after the next deploy. A real value already exists in this Mac's `apps/web/.env.local` — copy that one, or generate a fresh one (`openssl rand -hex 24`) and set it in both places.
>
> **✅ Spotify taste-data refresh pipeline built + verified live end-to-end (2026-07-09, Mac) — prep work for a future "Quick Add" tab, not shipped as a user-facing feature itself.** Root cause fixed: the Spotify refresh token was already being captured at login but only ever written to `UserDefaults`, never persisted server-side — so `profiles.spotify_artists`/`spotify_recently_played` silently stopped updating for good once the ~1hr access token expired (confirmed: 1 of 167 accounts had any data at all, 2+ weeks stale). New `spotify_taste_tokens` table (migration `20260709000000`, **already applied** by the user) + a new `CRON_SECRET`-gated route (`/api/cron/refresh-spotify-taste`, daily via root `vercel.json`) that redeems the token and re-fetches live Spotify data server-side. Verified with a real account, not just compiled: signed out/back in with Spotify on the simulator, confirmed a real refresh token landed in the new table, ran the cron route locally, and watched `spotify_artists` pick up a genuinely new artist and `spotify_recently_played` go from `null` to 5 real albums. Full writeup + design rationale in SESSIONS.md.
>
> **✅ Rating modal comment-box clipping fixed (2026-07-09, Mac).** Tapping "Add a comment" during post-rating grew the `TextEditor` past the sheet's fixed detent instead of resizing it — fixed in all three flows (`ManualRatingSheet`, `InstinctRatingView`, `InstinctTrackRatingView`) by measuring `PostRatingOptionsView`'s real content height live and feeding it into `.presentationDetents`. The instinct-mode flows turned out to be more broken than the manual one (their `onChange(of: phase)` was resetting the detent on every phase change, including into post-rating) — worth knowing since this Mac's test account defaults to Instinct mode, so that's the flow that actually reproduces user-facing bugs first. Verified live via idb. No action needed.
>
> **✅ Artist page shipped (2026-07-09, Mac): Albums filter/sort (default Newest), Community post/list toggle (default posts), split Popular Albums/Popular Songs stats, corrected Songs default sort (most-rated first, ties by newest).** No action needed — all client-side, no migration involved. One real bug caught along the way: the `get_artist_release_groups` RPC's `DISTINCT ON (rg.id) ... ORDER BY rg.id, rg.first_release_date DESC` doesn't actually sort by date in its output (Postgres orders `DISTINCT ON` results by the full `ORDER BY`, and `rg.id` leads it) — worked around client-side by sorting `Release`s by date in Swift rather than trusting the RPC's order; the RPC itself wasn't touched. See SESSIONS.md 2026-07-09 for full detail.

> **✅ APPLIED 2026-07-10 (Windows) — both advisor migrations are now live** (via `scripts/db-exec.ts`, each verified against `pg_policies` before/after). Full trace in SESSIONS.md 2026-07-10 (Windows).
> - **✅ `20260708000000` (critic_affiliation → `security_invoker`)** — applied clean; confirmed `reloptions = {security_invoker=true}`.
> - **✅ `20260708000001` (auth-InitPlan wrap)** — first run **failed loudly + atomically** (`BEGIN/COMMIT` rolled the whole thing back, nothing partial) on stale policy names for tables tracked only in the top-level `supabase/migrations` dir. Diffed the full live policy set up front and fixed 3 things against `pg_policies`: `list_item_tracks_insert`/`_delete` → real names `lit_insert`/`lit_delete`; `reports_insert` → `"users can report"`; and added `daily_answers_delete_own` (the file wrapped select/insert/update but missed delete). Re-ran clean. **Post-state: only 3 bare-`auth.` policies remain in all of `public`, and they're exactly the out-of-scope `releases` ones below.** (`daily_answers`/`daily_questions` naming was verified live — `apps/web`'s `_own` names ARE what's live, so no abort there.)
> - **✅ `20260710000000` (NEW — resolves the old `ratings` note below)** — confirmed `ratings` had **8** policies (4 tracked + 4 untracked `"Allow authenticated users to …"` created via SQL editor). Pulled all their `qual`/`with_check` and proved pairwise each untracked one is fully superseded by its tracked `ratings_*` counterpart (DELETE/INSERT identical; untracked SELECT `auth.uid()=user_id` is a strict subset of `ratings_select`'s `true` public-read → no visibility change; untracked UPDATE equals `ratings_update`). Dropped all 4 in a **tracked** migration → `ratings` now 4 policies, all confirmed wrapped.
> - **🟡 Follow-up found, NOT fixed (needs a decision):** the same untracked-duplicate pattern exists on **`releases`**. Its untracked INSERT/SELECT are redundant subsets of `releases_insert`/`releases_select` (both `true`), but `"Allow authenticated users to update releases"` is the **only** UPDATE policy on the table — no tracked `releases_update` exists — so it can't just be dropped without removing authenticated UPDATE entirely. Consolidating `releases` means first authoring a proper tracked `releases_update` (and deciding whether authenticated-anyone should be able to UPDATE catalog rows at all, vs. service-role-only). Left for a dedicated pass; also still-unwrapped by design: the optional `storage.objects` avatar block at the bottom of `20260708000001`.
> - **Still open (unchanged, not addressed by the above — a judgment call, not mechanical):** 6 tables (`mixes`, `mix_items`, `mix_likes`, `mix_shares`, `mix_share_likes`, `rating_likes`) each have both a `FOR ALL` (owner) and a separate `FOR SELECT` (public/broader) permissive policy — not redundant (each is a different visibility rule), but Postgres evaluates both per row, doubling cost even after the wrap fix. Merging them requires care to not change who can see what.
>
> **What was fixed this session (Mac, already shipped/built, no Windows action needed):** Explore feed's `fetchExplorePool()` was silently dropping all ratings on a bot-query timeout (split into a lighter query + separate release_group batch-fetch); the Rankings unlock gate was failing *open* instead of locked when its status RPC timed out (now fails locked); Search's three lookups ran sequentially so a slow song query delayed fast album/artist results (now concurrent, song lookup raced against a 2s timeout); Search's empty-results state didn't render at all for 1-character queries, collapsing the whole screen to just the search bar and centering it (now always renders, message differs by query length); Album detail page UI unified across manual/instinct modes + a long-press quick-actions menu (Rate/Add to Mix/Share/View Artist) added to every bare album-cover surface; Profile's Rated tab now defaults to Posts view (was List) for both own and other profiles, and its hero section now scrolls with the page instead of staying pinned.

> **What was built 2026-07-10 (Windows — backend caching layer for load stability):**
> - ✅ **Cached server routes now back the Home feed and Charts** — the "blank squares / unstable loading"

>   against the Micro instance. Three new routes give the silla-route treatment to the rest: **`/api/feed`**
>   (150-row explore pool + like/comment counts, Redis 60s + CDN swr), **`/api/charts/summary`** (unlock +
>   pulse + most-rated + trending, Redis 300s), **`/api/charts/songs`** (Redis 600s). All service-role,
>   rate-limited, and never cache a payload with an errored section. Pages fall back to their old direct
>   RPCs if a route fails, so a route outage can't blank a page. New `lib/sj/apiClient.ts` typed fetchers.
> - ✅ **`/api/img` proxy hardened** (see cover bullet above) — blank cover squares degrade to a slower
>   direct fetch instead of a broken image.

>   because migration `20260706000003` made the gauge bot-decayed (bot ratings damp toward 0 as real

> **What was built 2026-07-07/08 (Windows) + OPEN ITEMS:**
> - ✅ **Genre hierarchy DONE + verified live.** k-pop albums no longer leak into the Hip-Hop (or R&B/Electronic/Pop) charts. Journey: the `primary_genre` COLUMN on `release_groups` is **unfillable** — that table has an HNSW vector index + 7 GIN trgm indexes, so a 400-row UPDATE times out at 120s even on an idle instance. Fixed with a **side table `rg_primary_genre`** (`20260706000019` recreates all 5 genre RPCs to `LEFT JOIN` it; `20260706000020` trigger populates a rated album's row on first rating). Populated for the chart-eligible set (rated ∪ prestige = 3,644). **Verified: 0 k-pop-primary albums in the hip-hop chart, 500 in the k-pop chart.** Dead `release_groups.primary_genre` column + `backfill_primary_genre_proc` left unused.
> - ✅ **Silla leaderboard genre filter** also swapped to primary (`20260706000018`), timeout fix preserved.
> - ✅ **Merged the Mac's 21-commit push** (web rebuild + sessions 4–5); reconciled migration-number collisions (mine `000001/2` vs Mac's). `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` set on this device → `scripts/db-exec.ts` (Management API SQL) works here now.
> - ✅ **Cover proxy FIXED (07-09, `086451e`) — VERIFIED LIVE ON PROD 07-10** (`/api/img` returns 200 image/jpeg; 3.8s cold → 0.12s warm edge hits). The 502 was Vercel *edge* egress being rejected by archive.org (serverless IPs get through); `/api/img` moved edge→nodejs runtime + retries transient CAA cold-failures. Covers render on web now (the CSP was also missing `https://*.archive.org` for CAA's final redirect hop). **07-10:** proxy further hardened — per-attempt 6s abort timeout, 3 attempts with backoff, edge-cached 404s, and total failure now 302-redirects the browser to the direct URL instead of dying with a 502. The side-table URL-resolution idea is no longer needed. **Note for Mac:** session 7 (07-08, pre-fix) had re-diagnosed the proxy as broken and **reverted iOS `thumbnailUrl` to skip `/api/img`** — that revert is now safe to undo; **re-apply the `Theme.swift` proxy routing** so iOS gets instant edge-cached covers instead of 2.4s direct CAA loads.
> - ⚠️ **Lesson logged:** the "07-07 DB-load incident" (prod IO starvation) was **orphaned backfill processes** — `TaskStop` detached harness tasks without killing node procs, so ~4 runs wrote concurrently for hours. Kill backfills by PID (`Stop-Process`), never rely on `TaskStop`. Direct metrics confirmed it was disk IO, never CPU/RAM.
> - ⏳ **Blocked-by-HNSW (open):** `backfill:embeddings` (~20% of albums+EPs null + HNSW rebuild owed) and any cover backfill write to `release_groups` → same 120s-UPDATE wall. Need a maintenance window (drop indexes → bulk write → rebuild) or a side-table rethink.

> **Notification taps now actually navigate.** The tap-to-relevant-page logic (follow → profile, like/comment → album, mix likes/comments → mix) was already fully built in `NotificationsView`/`AppNotification`, but silently did nothing when tapped — root cause was `HomeView`'s `NavigationStack` mixing `.navigationDestination(isPresented:)` (presenting `NotificationsView` itself) with `.navigationDestination(for:)` handlers (for pushes from within it), which let the back button's title update to the right destination without ever completing the visual push. Fixed by converting the bell button to the same value-based `NavigationLink` pattern already used elsewhere in the file (`FindPeopleDestination`). Verified live via idb: both follow→profile and like→album now render correctly. **Follow-up:** albums reached via a notification then showed a permanently blank cover — unrelated to the CAA-proxy incident above, this was `NotificationsView`'s own query never selecting `release_groups.cover_url`, so `AppNotification.albumRelease` always built its `Release` with `coverUrl: nil`. Fixed by adding `cover_url` to that select.
>

A delete (trash) icon is now always visible next to Share instead of living inside the old sheet. `EditMixView.swift` deleted. Verified live via idb (typed into the title field, confirmed the DB write, reverted the test edit).

Measured live: full page content (header + Albums list) now renders in ~1.5s, down from ~2.5–4.5s. **Flagged but not done:** switching the song lookup to `primary_artist_id` (indexed) would fix the root slowness directly but would silently drop songs where the artist is a featured/secondary credit — needs a decision, not a silent fix. > **⚑ PREVIOUS SESSION (2026-07-07, Mac, session 5):**
>
> **TestFlight is live — Build 2 uploaded and processed.** `CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION` bumped 1→2 in `project.pbxproj` (had never been incremented before; App Store Connect needs a unique build number per upload). Two informational-only warnings on the delivery, neither blocking: (1) **Sentry symbols upload failed** — Sentry's SPM product ships as a precompiled XCFramework with no bundled dSYM, so Xcode's separate "upload symbols" sub-step fails; only affects Apple's ability to symbolicate frames *inside Sentry's own SDK*, not this app's crashes — widely reported as benign, no fix applied. (2) **ITMS-90892 missing 152×152 iPad icon** — cosmetic quirk of the modern single-1024px-icon workflow (`AppIcon.appiconset`), icon still renders fine on iPad — not fixed (optional legacy-icon cleanup, not a blocker). The actual blocker was **"Missing Compliance"** (export-compliance/encryption question unanswered) — resolved by answering **"None of the algorithms mentioned above"** (app only does standard HTTPS via `URLSession` + one `SHA256` hash via `CryptoKit` for the Sign in with Apple nonce — no custom/proprietary crypto). Added `ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption = false` to `Info.plist` so **future uploads skip this question automatically** (this only fixed it going forward — Build 2 itself needed the one-time manual answer in App Store Connect). Xcode Cloud discussed as a future automation option (auto-incrementing build number, no manual Archive/Organizer dance) — **not set up**, still manual upload.
>

New `ProfilePost` enum merges rating posts + mix-share posts by recency in the Posts display tab (list-mode/other sort orders unaffected — merge only applies under the default "Recent" sort, since top/bottom-rated and A–Z have no sensible slot for a score-less mix share). > - ⚠️ **Found + fixed mid-session: a 133GB Xcode RAM blowup.** Root cause: a `switch` with two multi-argument view initializers written *inline* inside a `ForEach`/`LazyVStack`/`ScrollView` closure (`HomeView.swift`'s `feedList`) — a known Swift type-checker explosion trigger (runaway memory instead of a fast error). Fixed by extracting into named `postCard`/`ratingCard`/`mixShareCard` functions — named function bodies type-check independently instead of blowing up as one giant nested expression. **Worth remembering if this happens again**: kill `swift-frontend` in Activity Monitor (not the whole Mac), then look for this exact shape (heterogeneous switch/if-else branches with many-arg initializers inline in a ViewBuilder closure) in whatever was just edited.
> - ✅ Username format also tightened this session (`20260706000014_username_format_constraint.sql`) — 3–20 chars, `[a-z0-9_]` only, now consistent across web onboarding/settings/`check-username` API and iOS onboarding/edit-profile (previously only one of six entry points validated this at all — web settings and both iOS paths had zero validation). Truncation/`lineLimit` guards added to ~9 UI spots (web + iOS) that could overflow on a long/legacy username, plus an OG-image truncation fallback. **✅ Migration applied** (confirmed, along with `20260706000015`).

> **What was built 2026-07-03 (Mac — iOS localization, en/ko, all 24 files):**
> - ✅ **Full iOS en/ko localization** — web has had this for a while; iOS had zero (everything hardcoded English), a real gap for a Korea-first product. `Localizable.xcstrings` String Catalog + Korean added as a project language. 305 catalog keys, reusing web's `ko.ts` glossary wherever terms overlap for cross-platform consistency.
> - Went well past copy-pasting translations — most of the actual work was finding and fixing Swift patterns that don't auto-localize even with a String Catalog present: reusable-view `String` params that needed retyping to `LocalizedStringKey` (6+ helper functions across files), enum `rawValue`s displayed directly (fixed via runtime `LocalizedStringKey`/`String.LocalizationValue` lookups), and 7 separate copies of a `?? "someone"` username-fallback pattern spread across the codebase. `Release.typeLabel` (one shared computed property returning "Album"/"EP"/"Single") was a single high-leverage fix that cascaded correctly to nearly every screen already using it.
> - **Deliberately left in English:** `RankingsView`'s genre/country filter chip labels — those values double as the actual backend filter parameter, so translating the display would've meant restructuring the filter logic too; a scoped trade-off, not an oversight.
> - Xcode's own build-time string extraction caught things the manual pass missed (a stray raw interpolation in `RankingsView.swift`, a few literal misses) — reconciled via a Python script operating on the `.xcstrings` JSON directly, since Xcode's own rebuild had reformatted the file. Two full-app builds succeeded. **Not yet visually verified with the simulator actually set to Korean — do that first next session** before considering this fully done.

> **What was built 2026-07-03 (Windows — Korean phonetic search + native-title source research):**
> - ✅ **Korean phonetic search (`드레이크`→Drake) — infra built.** New `artists.name_phonetic_ko` column (a *separate* column, deliberately not overloading `name_native`, per the handoff — `name_native` = an artist's actual native identity, `name_phonetic_ko` = the searchable Korean spelling of a non-Korean artist). Migration `20260703000006_artist_phonetic_ko.sql` adds the column + trigram index and rebuilds `search_artists` to match/rank it (RETURN signature unchanged → no iOS/web client changes). Backfill `scripts/backfill-phonetic-ko.ts` (`npm run backfill:phonetic-ko`) sources the rendering from Korean Wikipedia interlanguage links — public API, no scraping. **✅ Complete** — migration applied, backfill run: **3,566 artists** populated. Verified live via the `search_artists` RPC: 드레이크→Drake (outranks Drake Bell via exact-vs-prefix scoring), 라디오헤드→Radiohead, 위켄드→The Weeknd, 켄드릭 라마→Kendrick Lamar, 빌리 아일리시→Billie Eilish, 아리아나 그란데→Ariana Grande. Conservative (returns no-match rather than a wrong guess on ambiguous pages like Nirvana/YOASOBI). ~16% of non-Korean artists filled — the famous ones Koreans actually search; the obscure long tail has no Korean Wikipedia article. **One-shot** — artists ingested *after* this run won't auto-get a phonetic (future: populate `name_phonetic_ko` in `mb-ingest`). Also fixed a nested-paren `stripDisambig` bug that wrote one dirty value before being caught + reverted.
> - ✅ **Korean native album titles — precision backfill from Wikipedia (Deezer built but held).** The Korean streaming platforms (Melon/Bugs/Genie/Vibe) *all* `Disallow: /` for general crawlers in robots.txt — so no scraping. Instead: `scripts/backfill-native-titles-wiki.ts` (`npm run backfill:native-titles`) sources Korean album titles from EN Wikipedia `ko` langlinks with **three stacked guards** (exact title match, artist-category match, Hangul-only) so a wrong title can never be written ("A Flower Bookmark"→꽃갈피; "Palette"/"Love poem" correctly skipped as genuinely-Latin). A Deezer tier (`backfill-native-titles-deezer.ts`) was built with sound artist-scoped guards but **held un-run** — a dry-run showed ~0.2% yield and dirty title strings (e.g. "쏘리 쏘리 SORRY, SORRY - The 3rd Album"), failing the no-erroneous-data bar. **Key finding:** the raw "6,464 / 7,425 (87%) missing `native_title`" *overstates* the gap — most of those nulls are albums with legitimately **Latin** official titles (IU's Palette/LILAC/Love poem) that should stay Latin; the genuinely-Korean-titled ones were largely already captured at ingest (꽃갈피 stored in Korean). Wikipedia fills only the true residual, all verified.

> - ✅ **Root-cause + data-correction: wrong Korean artist names** (user caught E SENS showing his birth name "강민호" instead of no native name). Bug was in `scripts/mb-ingest.ts`'s `pickNative()` — grabbed *any* CJK alias when none was flagged primary, including unrelated birth/legal names. Fixed going forward (only trust a flagged-primary alias, else null). Correcting existing bad rows took 3 rounds of heuristic hardening in new `scripts/fix-bad-native-names.ts` (each round caught by spot-checking specific artists — see SESSIONS.md for the full trace: ENHYPEN, Lily Chou‐Chou, Korean-idol Hangul mononyms like 가은/윤상/지민, aiko). **Applied for real:** 626 corrections across 19,461 artists with alias data, spot-verified in production. **One known residual case** (`郭靜`→`郭伯瑜`) is an upstream MusicBrainz curation error (real name flagged `primary_for_locale: true` in their own data), not fixable by this script without cross-referencing an external source.
